[MD]  README.md
================================================================================
  1: # ModelMesh
  2: 
  3: A multi-model inference gateway. Clients authenticate, name a model, and either submit an **asynchronous job** (REST) or send a request over a **WebSocket**. The gateway looks the model up in a Postgres **registry** on every request, so adding, disabling, re-pointing or canarying a model is a data change.
  4: 
  5: It ships with two example workers, **`doc-ocr`** (EasyOCR) and **`asr`** (Whisper `tiny`), runs on Docker Compose or Kubernetes (minikube), and includes a Redis-Streams job queue with retries and crash recovery, per-key rate limiting, best-effort ClickHouse metrics, and an optional canary rollback guard.
  6: 
  7: > **Status: a development implementation, not production-ready.** The core path is tested (102 tests pass, reproduced) and the project is candid about its gaps. But there is no TLS, workers and Redis are unauthenticated, registration is open, a full Redis returns bare HTTP 500s while `/ready` stays green, and uploads linger in Redis for an hour. Read [Status and limitations](docs/status-and-limitations.md) and [Security](docs/security.md) before exposing it.
  8: 
  9: ## What it does
 10: 
 11: - **REST jobs.** `POST /v1/infer/{model}` stores the upload, queues a job, and returns a `job_id`; `GET /v1/jobs/{id}` returns the result. Jobs are private to the user who created them.
 12: - **WebSocket inference.** `/v1/ws/infer/{model}` sends one whole file and gets the result back on the same socket, calling the worker over HTTP or gRPC as the registry says.
 13: - **Registry-driven routing.** Name, version, worker, protocol, endpoint, status and canary percentage live in Postgres.
 14: - **Canary routing** on the REST path by version number, with a CLI guard that can reset a canary to 0%.
 15: - **Reliable workers.** Retries with 2, 4, 8 second backoff, a dead-letter stream, and recovery of jobs left by a crashed worker. Permanent input errors (bad image, bad audio) fail immediately with the real error.
 16: - **Auth.** Users with bcrypt passwords, JWTs for creating API keys, hashed API keys, per-key fixed-window rate limits, admin keys for registry changes.
 17: - **Best-effort metrics.** One ClickHouse row per finished job (Kubernetes, once the table is created).
 18: 
 19: ## What it does not do
 20: 
 21: - **No TLS, Ingress, autoscaling, Prometheus, Grafana, or `/metrics`.** None are built.
 22: - **No true streaming.** The "streaming" WebSocket sends the whole file as one message.
 23: - **No canary, retries, queueing or metrics on the WebSocket path.**
 24: - **No durable Redis on Kubernetes**, no job replay tool, no multi-tenancy, no result cache.
 25: - **No end-to-end test and no CI pipeline.**
 26: - The Kafka code is **dormant** and has drifted from the Redis path.
 27: 
 28: ## How it fits together
 29: 
 30: ```mermaid
 31: flowchart LR
 32:     C["Client"] -->|"REST job"| G["Gateway<br/>FastAPI :8000"]
 33:     C -->|"WebSocket"| G
 34:     G --> PG[("Postgres<br/>users, keys, registry")]
 35:     G --> RD[("Redis<br/>job stream, payloads,<br/>results, rate limits")]
 36:     RD --> W1["doc-ocr worker<br/>HTTP, gRPC, consumer"]
 37:     RD --> W2["asr worker<br/>HTTP, gRPC, consumer"]
 38:     G -.->|"WebSocket path:<br/>direct call"| W1
 39:     G -.-> W2
 40:     W1 -.->|"best effort"| CH[("ClickHouse<br/>Kubernetes only")]
 41:     W2 -.-> CH
 42: ```
 43: 
 44: Details: [Architecture](docs/architecture.md).
 45: 
 46: ## Quick start
 47: 
 48: Requires Docker with Compose (about 10 GB of disk; the **first build is slow**, about 51 minutes on the author's machine).
 49: 
 50: ```bash
 51: cp .env.example .env        # set JWT_SECRET to a long random value
 52: docker compose up --build -d
 53: curl -s http://localhost:8000/ready          # {"status":"ready"}
 54: ```
 55: 
 56: Then register, log in, create an API key and submit a job; the step-by-step commands, sample files, admin setup and the Kubernetes path are in [Getting started](docs/getting-started.md).
 57: 
 58: ## API at a glance
 59: 
 60: | Endpoint                                            | Auth                | Purpose                               |
 61: | --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
 62: | `POST /v1/auth/register`, `/login`, `/api-keys`     | none, none, JWT     | Accounts and API keys                 |
 63: | `GET /v1/models`, `/v1/models/{name}`               | none                | Public model listing (routing hidden) |
 64: | `POST /v1/models`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /v1/models/{id}` | admin key           | Manage the registry                   |
 65: | `POST /v1/infer/{model}`                            | API key             | Submit a job                          |
 66: | `GET /v1/jobs/{id}`                                 | API key (same user) | Poll a job                            |
 67: | `WS /v1/ws/infer/{model}`                           | API key in query    | Direct inference                      |
 68: | `GET /health`, `/ready`                             | none                | Liveness, readiness                   |
 69: 
 70: Full contract with captured responses: [API](docs/api.md).
 71: 
 72: ## Know this before you rely on it
 73: 
 74: Measured on a real Postgres 16 and Redis 7 during this documentation review:
 75: 
 76: - **A full Redis breaks uploads with HTTP 500.** With the Kubernetes setting (200 MB, no eviction), the 16th consecutive 10 MiB upload failed. `/ready` kept answering 200.
 77: - **WebSocket errors leak the internal worker URL** that `/v1/models` is built to hide.
 78: - **Finished jobs leave their upload in Redis for an hour.**
 79: - **A job for a model with no running worker is never processed and never fails.** It shows `queued` until it expires.
 80: - **The canary guard can roll back a healthy canary** right after it is enabled; wait one full window before running it with `--apply`.
 81: - The suite needs a real Postgres and Redis and **leaves rows behind**; use a scratch database.
 82: 
 83: ## Repository layout
 84: 
 85: ```
 86: gateway/          FastAPI gateway: main.py, auth, registry, job_queue, rate_limit,
 87:                   grpc_client, canary_guard.py (CLI), kafka_queue.py (dormant)
 88: workers/
 89:   doc_ocr/        EasyOCR worker: HTTP, gRPC, Redis consumer, metrics
 90:   asr/            Whisper worker: HTTP, gRPC (with PredictStream), consumer, metrics
 91: db/init.sql       Postgres schema and seed models
 92: migrations/       Idempotent schema changes (003, 004)
 93: clickhouse/       Metrics table DDL (reconstructed; apply by hand)
 94: k8s/              Namespace, Postgres, Redis, ClickHouse, gateway, workers
 95: docker-compose.yml, docker-compose.test.yml
 96: scripts/smoke_test.sh
 97: test_ws_client.py, test_ws_client.html, ws_latency_compare.py
 98: load-test.yml     Artillery scenario
 99: tests/            102 pytest tests (real Postgres and Redis required)
100: docs/             Documentation (below)
101: ```
102: 
103: Not in the reviewed export but referenced by the project: `inference.proto`, two Kubernetes Secret manifests, `.env.example`, `fixtures/`, `load-test-vars.csv`.
104:

120: ## Tests
121: 
122: ```bash
123: export TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost/modelmesh_test
124: export TEST_REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/1
125: pip install -r tests/requirements.txt
126: pytest -q                     # 102 passed, 8 warnings; run it twice
127: ```
128: 
129: Setup of the scratch database and Redis is in [Testing](docs/testing.md#running-the-tests).

[SQL]  clickhouse\init.sql
================================================================================
  1: -- RECONSTRUCTED from the insert columns and documented queries. Verify with SHOW CREATE TABLE.
  2: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS request_metrics (
  3:     timestamp     DateTime DEFAULT now(),
  4:     request_id    String,
  5:     model_id      String,
  6:     model_name    String,
  7:     model_version String,
  8:     worker_name   String,
  9:     status        String,
 10:     latency_ms    UInt32
 11: ) ENGINE = MergeTree
 12: ORDER BY (model_name, model_version, timestamp);
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[SQL]  db\init.sql
================================================================================
  1: -- Enable UUID generation
  2: CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS "pgcrypto";
  3: 
  4: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
  5:     id            UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  6:     email         TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  7:     password_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
  8:     created_at    TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
  9: );
 10: 
 11: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS api_keys (
 12:     id                 UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
 13:     user_id            UUID REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
 14:     key_hash           TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
 15:     rate_limit_per_min INT DEFAULT 60,
 16:     is_admin           BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
 17:     created_at         TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
 18:     revoked            BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE
 19: );
 20: 
 21: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS models (
 22:   id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
 23:   name TEXT NOT NULL,
 24:   version TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'v1',
 25:   worker_name TEXT NOT NULL,
 26:   protocol TEXT NOT NULL,
 27:   endpoint TEXT NOT NULL,
 28:   status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
 29:   supports_streaming BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
 30:   canary_percent INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
 31:   description TEXT,
 32:   created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now(),
 33:   CONSTRAINT models_protocol_chk CHECK (protocol IN ('http','grpc')),
 34:   CONSTRAINT models_status_chk CHECK (status IN ('active','disabled','deleted')),
 35:   CONSTRAINT models_canary_chk CHECK (canary_percent BETWEEN 0 AND 100),
 36:   UNIQUE(name, version)
 37: );
 38: 
 39: CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS requests (
 40:     id          UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
 41:     api_key_id  UUID REFERENCES api_keys(id),
 42:     model_id    UUID REFERENCES models(id),
 43:     model_type  TEXT NOT NULL,
 44:     status      TEXT NOT NULL,            -- 'success' | 'error' | 'rate_limited'
 45:     latency_ms  INT,
 46:     created_at  TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT now()
 47: );
 48: 
 49: -- Seed the two initial models
 50: INSERT INTO models (name, version, worker_name, protocol, endpoint, supports_streaming, description)
 51: VALUES
 52: ('doc-ocr', 'v1', 'doc-ocr-worker', 'http', 'http://doc-ocr-worker:8001/infer', FALSE, 'EasyOCR document text extraction'),
 53: ('asr', 'v1', 'asr-worker', 'http', 'http://asr-worker:8002/infer', TRUE, 'Whisper-tiny speech-to-text')
 54: ON CONFLICT (name, version) DO NOTHING;
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[YAML]  docker-compose.test.yml
================================================================================
  1: services:
  2:   postgres:
  3:     ports:
  4:       - "5432:5432"
  5:   redis:
  6:     ports:
  7:       - "6379:6379"
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\api.md
================================================================================
  1: # API
  2: 
  3: The gateway's HTTP and WebSocket contract. Every example below was **captured from a running gateway** against a real Postgres and Redis during this review. Identifiers are shortened (`…`) and tokens truncated; nothing else is edited. FastAPI also serves interactive documentation at `/docs`, `/redoc` and `/openapi.json`, **without authentication**.
  4: 
  5: | Endpoint                                  | Auth            | Purpose                                |
  6: | ----------------------------------------- | --------------- | -------------------------------------- |
  7: | `POST /v1/auth/register`                  | none            | Create a user                          |
  8: | `POST /v1/auth/login`                     | none            | Get a JWT                              |
  9: | `POST /v1/auth/api-keys`                  | JWT             | Create an API key                      |
 10: | `GET /v1/models`, `GET /v1/models/{name}` | **none**        | List or read models (internals hidden) |
 11: | `POST /v1/models`                         | admin API key   | Register a model version               |
 12: | `PATCH /v1/models/{model_id}`             | admin API key   | Update a model version                 |
 13: | `DELETE /v1/models/{model_id}`            | admin API key   | Disable a model version                |
 14: | `POST /v1/infer/{model_name}`             | API key         | Submit an asynchronous job             |
 15: | `GET /v1/jobs/{job_id}`                   | API key         | Poll a job                             |
 16: | `WS /v1/ws/infer/{model_name}`            | API key (query) | Direct inference over a WebSocket      |
 17: | `GET /health`, `GET /ready`               | none            | Liveness and readiness                 |
 18: 
 19: ## Conventions
 20: 
 21: - Base URL `http://localhost:8000`. There is no TLS, no CORS handling (a browser preflight returns `405`), and no `/metrics` endpoint.
 22: - Request and response bodies are JSON, except inference uploads, which are `multipart/form-data` with one field named `file`.
 23: - Errors use FastAPI's shape: `{ "detail": "<message>" }`, or for validation errors (`422`) `{ "detail": [ { "type", "loc", "msg", … } ] }`.
 24: - **Two credentials:**
 25:   - A **JWT** (24 hours, HS256) from `/v1/auth/login`. It is accepted **only** by `POST /v1/auth/api-keys`.
 26:   - An **API key** (random, shown once) sent as `Authorization: Bearer <key>`. It is accepted by everything else. Admin routes need a key with `is_admin = true`, which can only be set in the database ([Data stores](data-stores.md#making-a-key-an-administrator)).
 27: - The app sets no security headers (no `nosniff`, framing, CSP or HSTS).
 28: 
 29: ## Authentication
 30: 
 31: ### `POST /v1/auth/register`
 32: 
 33: ```json
 34: { "email": "user@example.com", "password": "at-least-8-chars" }
 35: ```
 36: 
 37: `200` → `{ "message": "User registered" }`. A duplicate email returns `400 {"detail": "Email already registered"}`. A password under 8 characters returns `422` (`"String should have at least 8 characters"`). The limit applies to new registrations only; older short passwords still work for login. There is no email verification and no rate limit on this endpoint.
 38: 
 39: ### `POST /v1/auth/login`
 40: 
 41: Same body. `200` → `{ "access_token": "eyJhbGciOiJI…", "token_type": "bearer" }`. Wrong credentials: `401 {"detail": "Invalid credentials"}`. Login is not rate limited.
 42: 
 43: ### `POST /v1/auth/api-keys`
 44: 
 45: Send `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>` and optionally `{ "rate_limit_per_min": 100 }` (default 60, allowed 1 to 1000).
 46: 
 47: `200` → `{ "key": "iMhjy9…", "rate_limit_per_min": 100 }`. **The raw key is returned once**; only its SHA-256 is stored. A value outside 1 to 1000 returns `422` (`"Input should be less than or equal to 1000"`). A missing or invalid token returns `401 {"detail": "Missing or invalid Authorization header"}`.
 48: 
 49: A user may create any number of keys, each with its own limit, so the per-key limit is not a per-user cap. There are no endpoints to list or revoke keys.
 50: 
 51: ## Models
 52: 
 53: ### `GET /v1/models` and `GET /v1/models/{name}?version=v1`
 54: 
 55: No authentication. The response **omits** `worker_name` and `endpoint` (the internal routing) but includes the model `id` and `canary_percent`. `GET /v1/models/{name}` defaults to `version=v1`.
 56: 
 57: ```json
 58: {
 59:   "id": "…",
 60:   "name": "asr",
 61:   "version": "v1",
 62:   "protocol": "http",
 63:   "status": "active",
 64:   "supports_streaming": true,
 65:   "canary_percent": 0,
 66:   "description": "Whisper-tiny speech-to-text"
 67: }
 68: ```
 69: 
 70: Unknown: `404 {"detail": "Unknown model"}`. Disabled models are still listed, with `"status": "disabled"`; deleted models are not.
 71: 
 72: ### `POST /v1/models` (admin)
 73: 
 74: ```json
 75: {
 76:   "name": "doc-ocr",
 77:   "version": "v2",
 78:   "worker_name": "doc-ocr-worker-v2",
 79:   "protocol": "http",
 80:   "endpoint": "http://doc-ocr-worker-v2:8001/infer",
 81:   "supports_streaming": false,
 82:   "canary_percent": 0,
 83:   "description": "candidate"
 84: }
 85: ```
 86: 
 87: `200` → `{ "id": "…", "status": "registered" }`.
 88: 
 89: | Field            | Rule                                   |
 90: | ---------------- | -------------------------------------- |
 91: | `version`        | Must match `^v\d+$` (default `v1`)     |
 92: | `protocol`       | `http` or `grpc`                       |
 93: | `canary_percent` | 0 to 100                               |
 94: | `endpoint`       | Any string; **not validated as a URL** |
 95: 
 96: Errors: `403 {"detail": "Admin key required"}` for a non-admin key; `409 {"detail": "Model doc-ocr:v2 already registered"}`; `422` for a bad version, protocol or percentage.
 97: 
 98: ### `PATCH /v1/models/{model_id}` (admin)
 99: 
100: Any of `worker_name`, `protocol`, `endpoint`, `status` (`active` or `disabled`), `supports_streaming`, `canary_percent`, `description`. `200` → `{ "status": "updated" }`. Invalid values return `422` (for example `"Input should be 'http' or 'grpc'"`). Null values are ignored, so a description cannot be cleared; `name` and `version` cannot be changed. An unknown ID returns `404`.
101: 
102: ### `DELETE /v1/models/{model_id}` (admin)
103: 
104: Soft delete: sets `status` to `disabled` and returns `{ "status": "disabled" }`. The row stays readable. New requests get `503 {"detail": "Model is currently disabled"}`.
105: 
106: How a version is chosen when none is given is described in [Registry and canary](registry-and-canary.md#how-a-model-version-is-chosen-rest).
107: 
108: ## Inference (asynchronous)
109: 
110: ### `POST /v1/infer/{model_name}?version=v2`
111: 
112: `multipart/form-data`, field `file`. Requires an API key.
113: 
114: ```http
115: POST /v1/infer/doc-ocr
116: Authorization: Bearer <api key>
117: ```
118: 
119: `200`:
120: 
121: ```json
122: {
123:   "job_id": "a583a59d-…",
124:   "status": "queued",
125:   "model": "doc-ocr",
126:   "version": "v1"
127: }
128: ```
129: 
130: Order of checks (this matters for which error you see and what counts against your limit):
131: 
132: 1. API key (`401`).
133: 2. **Rate limit** (`429`). This is counted even if the request fails a later check.
134: 3. Model lookup (`404`, `503`).
135: 4. Upload read and size check (`413`).
136: 5. Enqueue.
137: 
138: | Status | `detail`                                                                                                                                                              |
139: | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
140: | 401    | `Missing or invalid Authorization header` or `Invalid or revoked API key`                                                                                             |
141: | 404    | `Unknown model` (also for an unknown `?version=`)                                                                                                                     |
142: | 413    | `Upload exceeds maximum allowed size` (limit `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`, default 10 MiB; checked after the body is parsed)                                                    |
143: | 422    | Missing `file` field                                                                                                                                                  |
144: | 429    | `Rate limit exceeded` (no `Retry-After` or rate-limit headers)                                                                                                        |
145: | 503    | `Model is currently disabled`                                                                                                                                         |
146: | 500    | Unhandled error, for example **Redis refusing the write when it is full** (see [Data stores](data-stores.md#memory-limits-and-what-a-full-redis-looks-like-verified)) |
147: 
148: Pinning a version with `?version=` bypasses the canary split.
149: 
150: ### `GET /v1/jobs/{job_id}`
151: 
152: Requires an API key belonging to the **same user** that created the job.
153: 
154: ```json
155: {
156:   "job_id": "a583a59d-…",
157:   "status": "queued",
158:   "model_name": "doc-ocr",
159:   "model_version": "v1"
160: }
161: ```
162: 
163: Later responses: `{ "job_id", "status": "processing" }` while a worker holds the job (only the initial `queued` record carries the model name and version), then `{ "job_id", "status": "success", "result": { … } }` or `{ "job_id", "status": "failed", "error": "…" }`. Result shapes per model are in [Queue and workers](queue-and-workers.md#the-workers).
164: 
165: A missing job, an expired job and **another user's job all return the same** `404 {"detail": "Job not found or expired"}`. Results are kept about two hours, other job state about one hour.
166: 
167: ## WebSocket: `/v1/ws/infer/{model_name}`
168: 
169: Query parameters: `api_key` (required) and `version` (optional; **defaults to `v1`**, never uses the canary).
170: 
171: The server accepts the connection first, then validates. Flow:
172: 
173: 1. Server sends `{ "status": "ready", "model": "doc-ocr", "protocol": "http" }`.
174: 2. Client sends **one binary message** containing the whole file.
175: 3. Server replies, then closes:
176: 
177: | Path                                   | Reply                                                                            |
178: | -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
179: | HTTP worker, or gRPC without streaming | `{ "result": { … }, "is_final": true }`                                          |
180: | gRPC with `supports_streaming`         | One or more `{ "partial": "<text>", "is_final": <bool>, "confidence": <float> }` |
181: 
182: Rejections, each sent as `{ "error": "<message>" }` followed by a close (all verified):
183: 
184: | Case                             | Message                           | Close code          |
185: | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ------------------- |
186: | No `api_key`                     | `Missing api_key query parameter` | 4401                |
187: | Bad or revoked key               | `Invalid API key`                 | 4401                |
188: | Unknown model or version         | `Unknown model`                   | 4404                |
189: | Model disabled                   | `Model is currently disabled`     | 4503                |
190: | Rate limited                     | `429: Rate limit exceeded`        | 1000 (normal close) |
191: | Message above `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` | none                              | 1009                |
192: 
193: Notes:
194: 
195: - **Credentials travel in the URL.** The gateway redacts `api_key=` in its own access logs, but proxies and browsers may not.
196: - **Unexpected errors are returned verbatim** as `{ "error": "<exception text>" }`. Verified: when an HTTP worker returns 500, the client receives `Server error '500 Internal Server Error' for url 'http://<internal worker address>/infer'`, which discloses the worker address that `/v1/models` deliberately hides.
197: - The WebSocket path is **not queued, not retried, not metered and does not use the canary.**
198: - "Streaming" means the gRPC `PredictStream` call with a single final chunk; see [Queue and workers](queue-and-workers.md#grpc-servers).
199: - A reference client is `test_ws_client.py` (`python test_ws_client.py <api_key> <model> <file>`); it exits non-zero on any error or timeout. `test_ws_client.html` is a browser version.
200: 
201: ## Health
202: 
203: - `GET /health` → `200 {"status": "ok"}` always; it checks nothing.
204: - `GET /ready` → `200 {"status": "ready"}` after a `SELECT 1` on Postgres and a `PING` on Redis; otherwise `503` with `Not ready: Postgres unavailable` or `Not ready: Redis unavailable`. It does **not** detect a full Redis.
205: 
206: ## Rate limiting
207: 
208: Per API key, a **fixed 60-second window** that starts at the key's first request and is not extended by later ones. The counter increments atomically (`SET … NX EX 60` then `INCR`, in one transaction). Exceeding `rate_limit_per_min` returns `429`. Notes:
209: 
210: - Rate-limited, unknown-model and oversize requests all still count.
211: - A burst at the end of one window and the start of the next can reach twice the limit.
212: - No `Retry-After` or `X-RateLimit-*` headers are sent.
213: - If Redis is down the check raises and the request returns `500` (there is no fail-open).
214: - Registration and login are not limited.
215: 
216: ## Limits
217: 
218: | Limit                                             | Value                                                   |
219: | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
220: | Upload size (REST and WebSocket)                  | `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`, default 10,485,760                  |
221: | `rate_limit_per_min`                              | 1 to 1000 per key                                       |
222: | Password length                                   | at least 8 (new registrations)                          |
223: | JWT lifetime                                      | 24 hours                                                |
224: | Job state lifetime                                | about 1 hour (status, payload); 2 hours (result, owner) |
225: | gRPC call timeout                                 | 30 s (`Predict`), 60 s (`PredictStream`)                |
226: | HTTP call from gateway to worker (WebSocket path) | 30 s                                                    |
227: 
228: ## Quirks worth knowing
229: 
230: - `GET /v1/models/{name}` and `PATCH/DELETE /v1/models/{model_id}` share a path shape. A model **ID** passed to `GET` is treated as a name and returns `404`.
231: - `GET /v1/models` is public and shows `canary_percent` and model IDs.
232: - An unauthenticated caller can reach `/docs`, `/openapi.json` and register accounts.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\architecture.md
================================================================================
  1: # Architecture
  2: 
  3: How ModelMesh is put together and why. Queue and worker internals are in [Queue and workers](queue-and-workers.md); the model registry and canary logic are in [Registry and canary](registry-and-canary.md); storage is in [Data stores](data-stores.md).
  4: 
  5: ## What it is
  6: 
  7: A FastAPI gateway in front of model workers. Clients authenticate, pick a model **by name**, and either submit an asynchronous job (REST) or stream a request over a WebSocket. The gateway looks the model up in a Postgres **registry** at request time, so adding, disabling or re-pointing a model is a data change, not a code change.
  8: 
  9: Two example workers ship with it: `doc-ocr` (EasyOCR) and `asr` (Whisper `tiny`). Everything else (auth, rate limiting, queueing, canary routing, metrics) is generic and does not know which models exist.
 10: 
 11: The design goals visible in the code:
 12: 
 13: 1. **Registry-driven routing.** Model name, version, protocol, endpoint, status and canary percentage live in Postgres.
 14: 2. **Asynchronous by default.** REST inference returns a job ID immediately and a worker picks the job up from a Redis stream.
 15: 3. **Never lose a job silently where avoidable.** Retries with backoff, a dead-letter stream, and recovery of jobs left behind by a crashed worker.
 16: 4. **Observability must not break inference.** Metric writes are best-effort and back off after a failure.
 17: 5. **Honest scope.** Features that are not built (Prometheus, Grafana, TLS, Ingress, autoscaling) are documented as absent, not implied.
 18: 
 19: ## System overview
 20: 
 21: ```mermaid
 22: flowchart LR
 23:     C["Client"] -->|"REST: POST /v1/infer"| G
 24:     C -->|"WebSocket: /v1/ws/infer"| G
 25:     subgraph G["Gateway (FastAPI, port 8000)"]
 26:         AUTH["Auth: JWT and API keys"]
 27:         RL["Rate limit"]
 28:         REG["Registry lookup and canary pick"]
 29:     end
 30:     G --> PG[("Postgres<br/>users, API keys,<br/>model registry")]
 31:     G --> RD[("Redis<br/>stream, payloads,<br/>results, rate limits")]
 32:     RD -->|"XREADGROUP"| W1
 33:     RD -->|"XREADGROUP"| W2
 34:     subgraph W1["doc-ocr worker container"]
 35:         H1["HTTP /infer"]
 36:         R1["gRPC server"]
 37:         Q1["Redis consumer"]
 38:     end
 39:     subgraph W2["asr worker container"]
 40:         H2["HTTP /infer"]
 41:         R2["gRPC server"]
 42:         Q2["Redis consumer"]
 43:     end
 44:     G -.->|"WebSocket path only:<br/>HTTP or gRPC call"| H1
 45:     G -.-> R1
 46:     G -.-> H2
 47:     G -.-> R2
 48:     Q1 -.->|"best effort"| CH[("ClickHouse<br/>Kubernetes only")]
 49:     Q2 -.-> CH
 50: ```
 51: 
 52: Solid lines are the REST job path. Dotted lines are the WebSocket path (the gateway calls the worker directly) and the optional metrics write.
 53: 
 54: ## Components
 55: 
 56: | Component    | Where                                                   | Responsibility                                                                                           |
 57: | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 58: | Gateway      | `gateway/main.py` and helpers                           | HTTP and WebSocket API, auth, rate limiting, registry reads and writes, job enqueue, job status          |
 59: | Registry     | `gateway/registry.py`, Postgres `models` table          | Model records: name, version, worker name, protocol, endpoint, status, streaming support, canary percent |
 60: | Job queue    | `gateway/job_queue.py`, Redis                           | Stores the payload, owner and status, and appends a job to the shared stream `inference_jobs`            |
 61: | Workers      | `workers/doc_ocr/`, `workers/asr/`                      | Each container runs three processes: an HTTP server, a gRPC server and a Redis consumer                  |
 62: | Canary guard | `gateway/canary_guard.py`                               | Optional CLI that reads ClickHouse metrics and can reset `canary_percent` to 0                           |
 63: | Kafka code   | `gateway/kafka_queue.py`, `workers/*/kafka_consumer.py` | **Dormant.** Not imported, not deployed, and it has diverged from the Redis path                         |
 64: 
 65: ## Request path 1: asynchronous job (REST)
 66: 
 67: ```mermaid
 68: sequenceDiagram
 69:     autonumber
 70:     participant C as Client
 71:     participant G as Gateway
 72:     participant P as Postgres
 73:     participant R as Redis
 74:     participant W as Worker consumer
 75:     C->>G: POST /v1/infer/{model} (file, Bearer API key)
 76:     G->>P: look up key (hash, not revoked)
 77:     G->>R: rate-limit counter for the key
 78:     G->>P: resolve model (canary pick if no ?version=)
 79:     G->>G: read upload, reject above MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES (413)
 80:     G->>R: payload key, owner key, status key (all with TTL)
 81:     G->>R: XADD inference_jobs
 82:     G-->>C: 200 { job_id, status: queued }
 83:     W->>R: XREADGROUP (one job at a time)
 84:     W->>R: GET payload, run inference
 85:     W->>R: write result key, ACK
 86:     C->>G: GET /v1/jobs/{job_id}
 87:     G->>R: owner check, read result or status
 88:     G-->>C: status, result or error
 89: ```
 90: 
 91: Details that matter:
 92: 
 93: - The **worker is chosen from the registry's `worker_name`**. The REST path does not read the registry's `protocol` or `endpoint`; the workers pull from Redis.
 94: - The upload size limit is checked **after** FastAPI has parsed the multipart body. There is no HTTP-level body limit in the code.
 95: - A job belongs to the **user** who created it. Other users get `404`, the same as for an unknown or expired job.
 96: 
 97: ## Request path 2: WebSocket (direct)
 98: 
 99: ```mermaid
100: sequenceDiagram
101:     autonumber
102:     participant C as Client
103:     participant G as Gateway
104:     participant W as Worker (HTTP or gRPC)
105:     C->>G: connect /v1/ws/infer/{model}?api_key=...
106:     G->>G: accept, then check the key (close 4401 if bad)
107:     G->>G: rate limit, registry lookup (version defaults to v1)
108:     G-->>C: { status: ready, model, protocol }
109:     C->>G: ONE binary message (whole file)
110:     alt protocol is grpc and model supports streaming
111:         G->>W: PredictStream (one final chunk)
112:         W-->>G: partial results
113:         G-->>C: { partial, is_final, confidence }
114:     else HTTP, or gRPC without streaming
115:         G->>W: POST /infer or Predict
116:         W-->>G: result
117:         G-->>C: { result, is_final: true }
118:     end
119: ```
120: 
121: How it differs from the REST path:
122: 
123: |                                         | REST job                             | WebSocket                                                     |
124: | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
125: | Result                                  | Poll `GET /v1/jobs/{id}`             | Returned on the same socket                                   |
126: | Worker call                             | Redis stream, consumed by the worker | Gateway calls the worker's HTTP or gRPC endpoint **directly** |
127: | Uses registry `protocol` and `endpoint` | No (uses `worker_name`)              | Yes                                                           |
128: | Canary routing                          | Yes, when no `?version=`             | **No.** Defaults to `v1` unless a version is given            |
129: | Retries, DLQ, metrics                   | Yes                                  | **None**                                                      |
130: | Auth                                    | `Authorization: Bearer`              | `?api_key=` query parameter                                   |
131: | Input                                   | One multipart file                   | One binary message, whole file                                |
132: 
133: "Streaming" is narrower than the name suggests: the client sends the **entire file as one message**, and the ASR gRPC stream receives exactly one final chunk. See [Queue and workers](queue-and-workers.md#grpc-servers).
134: 
135: ## Worker container process model
136: 
137: ```mermaid
138: flowchart TD
139:     E["entrypoint.sh<br/>compiles protobuf stubs, then starts three processes"] --> A["uvicorn main:app<br/>HTTP :8001 or :8002"]
140:     E --> B["python grpc_server.py<br/>gRPC :50051"]
141:     E --> C["python consumer.py<br/>Redis consumer"]
142:     A -.->|"any process exits"| X["entrypoint kills the others<br/>and exits 1"]
143:     B -.-> X
144:     C -.-> X
145:     X --> RS["Compose: restart on-failure<br/>Kubernetes: container restart"]
146: ```
147: 
148: Consequences:
149: 
150: - **Each process loads its own copy of the model.** The ASR image loads Whisper in all three; the OCR image creates an EasyOCR reader at import in all three. Memory is therefore roughly three times a single model instance (see measured figures in [Operations](operations.md#resource-sizing)).
151: - **A dead sub-process takes the container down**, so a half-working worker restarts instead of lingering.
152: - Each consumer handles **one job at a time**. Throughput scales with the number of replicas, not with threads.
153: - The container runs as root; there is no `USER` instruction in any Dockerfile.
154: 
155: ## Deployment shapes
156: 
157: |                                           | Docker Compose                                                              | Kubernetes (minikube-oriented)                                                                                      |
158: | ----------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
159: | Services                                  | gateway, doc-ocr, asr, Postgres 16, Redis 7                                 | The same, plus ClickHouse; namespace `modelmesh`                                                                    |
160: | Metrics                                   | Off (ClickHouse is not in Compose)                                          | On, once the table DDL is applied by hand                                                                           |
161: | Postgres                                  | Named volume; `init.sql` runs only on a **fresh** volume                    | StatefulSet with a 1 Gi PVC; `init.sql` from a ConfigMap                                                            |
162: | Redis                                     | Volume `redisdata`                                                          | **Deployment with no volume**: queue, results and payloads are lost on pod restart. `maxmemory 200mb`, `noeviction` |
163: | Secrets                                   | Plain values in the Compose file; `JWT_SECRET` from `.env` or a placeholder | Dev Secret manifests committed to the repo (not for production)                                                     |
164: | Ingress, TLS, autoscaling, network policy | None                                                                        | None                                                                                                                |
165: 
166: Kubernetes manifests: 14 objects (namespace, 6 Services, 5 Deployments, 1 StatefulSet, 1 ConfigMap) plus two Secret manifests that were not part of the reviewed export.
167: 
168: ## Decisions and trade-offs
169: 
170: **One shared stream, one consumer group per worker.** All models write to `inference_jobs`; each worker's group reads every entry and acknowledges-and-skips entries meant for a different `worker_name`. This makes adding a worker a data change, at the cost that every worker reads every job. It also drove two rules: `XDEL` after `XACK` is **not** used (another group may not have read the entry), and `MAXLEN` trimming can drop unread entries if a backlog ever exceeds the limit.
171: 
172: **Payload outside the stream.** The uploaded bytes sit under `payload:{job_id}` with a TTL, not in the stream entry, so retries and the DLQ do not copy the file. The price is that payloads stay in Redis until they expire, even after a job succeeds.
173: 
174: **Registry-driven canary, not replica ratios.** The split is a probability applied per request in the gateway. It works with any orchestrator, but it only governs the REST path.
175: 
176: **Best-effort metrics.** One insert attempt per job on its **final** outcome; after a failure each worker skips attempts for 60 seconds. Observability outages never fail a job, but rows can be lost.
177: 
178: **Process isolation inside one container.** HTTP, gRPC and consumer are separate processes so one failure is visible to the supervisor, at the cost of three model copies.
179: 
180: **A new gRPC channel per call.** Deliberately not pooled, so calls spread across worker replicas behind a Kubernetes Service.
181: 
182: **Scope kept small.** Prometheus, Grafana, a result cache, a Postgres request log and a sliding-window rate limiter were documented in early drafts but never built; the decision was to correct the documents, not build them.
183: 
184: ## Code map
185: 
186: | Path                                                                                   | What is there                                                         |
187: | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
188: | `gateway/main.py`                                                                      | All HTTP and WebSocket routes, canary pick, `route_request`           |
189: | `gateway/auth.py`, `rate_limit.py`, `schemas.py`, `db.py`                              | Auth, limiter, request models, connections                            |
190: | `gateway/registry.py`, `job_queue.py`, `grpc_client.py`                                | Registry SQL, Redis enqueue and job status, gRPC calls                |
191: | `gateway/canary_guard.py`                                                              | Standalone guard CLI and its pure `decide()` function                 |
192: | `workers/<name>/main.py, grpc_server.py, consumer.py, inference.py, metrics.py`        | The three processes, the model, the metric writer                     |
193: | `db/init.sql`, `migrations/`, `clickhouse/init.sql`                                    | Schemas and idempotent migrations                                     |
194: | `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.test.yml`, `k8s/`                                | Deployment definitions                                                |
195: | `scripts/smoke_test.sh`, `test_ws_client.py`, `ws_latency_compare.py`, `load-test.yml` | Smoke test, WebSocket client, latency comparison, Artillery load test |
196: | `tests/`                                                                               | 102 pytest tests; see [Testing](testing.md)                           |
197: 
198: Size: about 4,400 lines of Python (gateway 1,242, workers 1,119, tests 1,768).
199: 
200: ## Things to know before changing code
201: 
202: - The two worker `consumer.py` files are near-identical copies (about 240 lines each). Change both.
203: - The two worker `metrics.py` files are identical.
204: - `gateway/main.py` and `canary_guard.py` each contain their own copy of the "highest numeric version" rule.
205: - `inference.proto` is the contract between gateway and workers. It was **not in the reviewed export**; the gRPC messages documented here were inferred from the code.
206: - Several files assume the generated protobuf modules exist: the gateway Dockerfile compiles them at **build** time, the worker entrypoints at **container start**.

 23: ### Architecture
 24: 
 25: ```
 26: Client → Gateway (FastAPI) → Worker (HTTP)
 27:          ↓
 28:          PostgreSQL (users, API keys, models; requests table is not currently written)
 29:          Redis (rate limiting; no result cache)
 30: ```
 31:

 41: - [x] Error handling (404 unknown, 503 disabled, 401 auth, 429 rate limit)
 42: - [x] The pytest suite passes after the Phase 7 test-harness repairs; the latest recorded review-follow-up run reported 102 passed, 8 warnings (see the [Phase 11 completion report](Phase11_Completion_Report.md))
 43: 
 44: ---
 45: 
 46: ## Key Design Decisions
 47: 
 48: **Model Registry Pattern**: The gateway resolves model metadata and asynchronous worker assignment from the database. HTTP routing sends octet-stream payloads and worker implementations perform model-specific decoding. The WebSocket path reads protocol from the registry; REST jobs do not.
 49: 
 50: **Trade-off**: One extra DB query per request. Worth it for flexibility gained in later phases.
 51: 
 52: ---
 53:

 55: 
 56: - `gateway/main.py` - FastAPI app with auth + routing
 57: - `gateway/auth.py` - JWT + API key verification
 58: - `gateway/rate_limit.py` - Redis-based rate limiting
 59: - `gateway/registry.py` - Model Registry service
 60: - `gateway/schemas.py` - Pydantic models
 61: - `gateway/db.py` - Database connection
 62: - `workers/doc_ocr/inference.py` - EasyOCR wrapper
 63: - `workers/asr/inference.py` - Whisper wrapper
 64: - `db/init.sql` - Database schema
 65: - `docker-compose.yml` - Service orchestration
 66: - `tests/*` - Auth, registry, worker tests
 67: 
 68: ---
 69: 
 70: ## Next Phase
 71:

[MD]  docs\archive\Phase11_Canary_Rollback_Guard.md

  1: # Phase 11: Canary Rollback Guard (optional, gated)
  2: 
  3: > **Historical implementation prompt.** Phase 11 has been completed and merged into `dev`. This file describes the original gated task, not a current completion checklist. See the [Phase 11 completion report](Phase11_Completion_Report.md) and the current [SETUP instructions](../SETUP.md#canary-rollback-guard).
  4: 
  5: > Paste this whole file into your coding assistant, inside the ModelMesh repo.
  6: > Global rules and the report format are in `00_Run_The_Project_Prompt.md`, Section A. They apply here.
  7: 
  8: **This is the only phase that adds a capability.** It is optional (decision D5). If you skip it, Phases 6 to 10 are still a complete plan.
  9: 
 10: **Goal:** if a canary version is clearly worse than the stable one, set its `canary_percent` to 0 automatically.
 11: **Why this feature:** it uses only data and endpoints that already exist (ClickHouse metrics, `GET /v1/models`, `PATCH /v1/models/{id}`), and your Phase 5 doc already lists it under "what would change with more time".
 12: **Effort:** 1.5 to 2 days.
 13: 
 14: ## Gate: check before writing any code
 15: 
 16: Stop and tell the human if any of these is false. Do not start the phase without all of them.
 17: 
 18: - [ ] Phases 6 to 10 reports exist, all exit criteria met, tag `phase-10-complete`, `pytest -q` green.
 19: - [ ] The human has confirmed they want Phase 11 (decision D5).
 20: - [ ] `request_metrics` has rows for **both** workers (Phase 10 verification).
 21: - [ ] `WORKER_NAME` comes from the environment (Phase 9, Task 9.5), so a v2 worker can run from the same image.
 22: - [ ] You have an **admin API key** for the gateway (Phase 0, step 0.6).
 23: 
 24: **Scope fence:** one new script, its tests, docs, and an optional CronJob. **Explicitly out of scope:** latency-based rules, auto-promote, statistical significance tests, notifications, a UI, multi-model orchestration, changes to gateway request handling, any change to the registry schema or API. Decision D6 stands: the rollback action is `canary_percent = 0` **only**. Never disable or delete v2.
 25: 
 26: ### Intended behaviour changes (everything else must stay identical)
 27: 
 28: - New: the guard script. It changes nothing unless run with `--apply`, and then only sets a canary's `canary_percent` to 0.
 29: - The gateway request path, the workers and the registry API are **not** touched, so the smoke `CHECK` lines must be identical to the baseline.
 30: 
 31: **Branch:** `phase-11-canary-guard`
 32: 
 33: ---
 34: 
 35: ## Design (follow it; do not redesign)
 36: 
 37: A standalone script `gateway/canary_guard.py`. It lives in `gateway/` so it ships inside the existing gateway image, which already has `httpx` and `clickhouse-connect` in its requirements. It is **not** imported by the app.
 38: 
 39: ### Inputs
 40: 
 41: | Name                   | Source                                   | Default                             |
 42: | ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
 43: | Gateway URL            | env `GATEWAY_URL`                        | `http://localhost:8000`             |
 44: | Admin API key          | env `ADMIN_KEY`                          | none (required only with `--apply`) |
 45: | ClickHouse host / port | env `CLICKHOUSE_HOST`, `CLICKHOUSE_PORT` | `clickhouse` / `8123`               |
 46: | Window                 | `--window-min`                           | 10                                  |
 47: | Minimum requests       | `--min-requests`                         | 30                                  |
 48: | Error-rate margin      | `--error-delta`                          | 0.10                                |
 49: | Apply changes          | `--apply`                                | off (dry-run)                       |
 50: 
 51: Never print the admin key. Never pass it on the command line.
 52: 
 53: ### Steps per run
 54: 
 55: 1. `GET {GATEWAY_URL}/v1/models` (public endpoint). Group rows by model `name`. For each name that has **two or more active versions** and at least one with `canary_percent > 0`: the **canary** is the highest numeric version (same rule as the gateway: the first number found in the version string), the **stable** is the lowest. Versions in between are ignored. Use the canary row's `canary_percent`.
 56: 2. Query ClickHouse once per model:
 57: 
 58: ```sql
 59: SELECT model_version, count() AS n, countIf(status = 'error') / count() AS err
 60: FROM request_metrics
 61: WHERE model_name = {name:String} AND timestamp > now() - INTERVAL {window:UInt32} MINUTE
 62: GROUP BY model_version
 63: ```
 64: 
 65: with `parameters={"name": ..., "window": ...}`. A version with no rows has `Stats(n=0, err=0.0)`. 3. Decide with a **pure function** (no I/O):
 66: 
 67: ```python
 68: from dataclasses import dataclass
 69: 
 70: @dataclass
 71: class Stats:
 72:     n: int
 73:     err: float
 74: 
 75: @dataclass
 76: class Config:
 77:     min_requests: int = 30
 78:     error_delta: float = 0.10
 79: 
 80: def decide(stable: Stats, canary: Stats, canary_percent: int, cfg: Config) -> tuple[str, str]:
 81:     if canary_percent == 0:
 82:         return "noop", "canary already at 0"
 83:     if stable.n < cfg.min_requests:
 84:         return "hold", "not enough stable traffic"
 85:     expected = stable.n * canary_percent / max(1, 100 - canary_percent)
 86:     if expected >= cfg.min_requests and canary.n < 0.2 * expected:
 87:         return "rollback", f"canary starved: {canary.n} completed vs about {expected:.0f} expected"
 88:     if canary.n < cfg.min_requests:
 89:         return "hold", "not enough canary traffic"
 90:     if canary.err - stable.err > cfg.error_delta:
 91:         return "rollback", f"canary error rate {canary.err:.2f} vs stable {stable.err:.2f}"
 92:     return "hold", "healthy"
 93: ```
 94: 
 95: 4. On `rollback` **and** `--apply`: `PATCH {GATEWAY_URL}/v1/models/{canary_id}` with `{"canary_percent": 0}` and header `Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_KEY`. On any other decision, or without `--apply`, change nothing.
 96: 5. Print **one JSON line per model per run** and nothing else on stdout:
 97: 
 98: ```json
 99: {
100:   "ts": "...",
101:   "model": "asr",
102:   "stable": "v1",
103:   "canary": "v2",
104:   "canary_percent": 20,
105:   "stable_stats": { "n": 140, "err": 0.01 },
106:   "canary_stats": { "n": 2, "err": 0.0 },
107:   "decision": "rollback",
108:   "reason": "canary starved: ...",
109:   "applied": true
110: }
111: ```
112: 
113: Exit code 0 normally. Exit non-zero only when the guard itself fails (gateway or ClickHouse unreachable, PATCH rejected). A failure must never leave a half-applied change.
114: 
115: ### Why these two rules
116: 
117: - **Starvation rule.** With the async queue, a canary whose worker is down or crashed does **not** produce errors. Its jobs sit in the stream unprocessed, so an error-rate-only guard would never fire. The starvation rule compares the canary's _completed_ count with what the configured percentage predicts from stable traffic (`stable * p / (100 - p)`).
118: - **Delta, not an absolute threshold.** If both versions are failing (for example Redis trouble), rolling back the canary would not help, so the guard compares against stable.
119: 
120: ### Known limitations (put these in the docs)
121: 
122: - A rolled-back canary's queued jobs stay unprocessed until their payload and status keys expire; clients polling those job ids eventually see a 404.
123: - The window is a sliding look-back, not an exact count since the canary started.
124: - If stable traffic is thin the guard holds; it never rolls back on thin data.
125: 
126: ---
127: 
128: ## Task 11.1: Write `gateway/canary_guard.py`
129: 
130: Requirements:
131: 
132: - `decide()`, `Stats` and `Config` exactly as above, importable without any third-party module.
133: - Import `clickhouse_connect` **inside** the function that queries ClickHouse, so the unit tests do not need it.
134: - A small `main()` with `argparse` for the flags above. Dry-run is the default.
135: - Use `httpx` for the two HTTP calls with a 10-second timeout.
136: - No retries, no loops, no sleeping: one run, then exit. Repetition is the CronJob's job.
137: - No new third-party dependency.
138: 
139: Commit: `phase11: canary guard script`.
140: 
141: ## Task 11.2: Unit tests for `decide()`
142: 
143: Create `tests/test_canary_guard.py`. The gateway directory is already on `sys.path` in `conftest.py`, so `import canary_guard` works. These tests need no database.
144: 
145: Required cases (use defaults `min_requests=30`, `error_delta=0.10` unless stated):
146: 
147: | Case                         | stable          | canary         | percent                      | Expected decision                                   |
148: | ---------------------------- | --------------- | -------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
149: | Already rolled back          | any             | any            | 0                            | `noop`                                              |
150: | Thin stable traffic          | n=10            | n=10           | 20                           | `hold`                                              |
151: | Healthy                      | n=200, err 0.01 | n=50, err 0.02 | 20                           | `hold`                                              |
152: | Error spike                  | n=200, err 0.01 | n=50, err 0.30 | 20                           | `rollback`                                          |
153: | Starved canary (worker down) | n=200, err 0.01 | n=0            | 20                           | `rollback`                                          |
154: | Not enough canary data       | n=200, err 0.01 | n=10, err 0.50 | 5 (expected=10.5, below min) | `hold`                                              |
155: | Both versions failing        | n=200, err 0.40 | n=50, err 0.45 | 20                           | `hold`                                              |
156: | Boundary on the margin       | n=200, err 0.10 | n=50, err 0.20 | 20                           | `hold` (difference equals the margin, not above it) |
157: 
158: Also test the model-selection helper (canary = highest numeric version, stable = lowest, middle ignored; `v9` vs `v10` picks `v10` as canary).
159: 
160: Commit: `phase11: unit tests for the guard decision`.
161:

163: 
164: 1. Dry-run first, from the Ubuntu WSL terminal with `modelmesh-env` active. The script needs `httpx` and `clickhouse-connect` (`pip install httpx clickhouse-connect` if missing, after a `--dry-run` check as in Phase 7). Use the Phase 0 key, which was promoted to admin, and do not echo it:
165: 
166: ```bash
167: source modelmesh-env/bin/activate
168: export ADMIN_KEY="$API_KEY"          # the Phase 0 key, already promoted to admin
169: cd gateway
170: GATEWAY_URL=http://localhost:8000 CLICKHOUSE_HOST=localhost python canary_guard.py
171: cd .. && deactivate
172: ```
173: 
174: For a Compose setup, use the temporary ClickHouse from Phase 10 Task 10.1 (it already publishes port 8123 to `localhost`). With no canary configured, the guard must print nothing and exit 0.
175: 
176: 2. Confirm dry-run never changes anything: re-read `GET /v1/models` afterwards.
177: 
178: ## Task 11.4: Demo plan (this is the honest version)
179: 
180: A genuinely failing worker would need test-only code inside the product, which is out of scope. So:
181: 
182: **Demo A: starvation (real).** This needs no new worker at all.
183: 
184: ```bash
185: # register a v2 whose worker does not exist, with a 20% canary
186: curl -s -X POST $BASE/v1/models -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_KEY" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
187:   -d '{"name":"asr","version":"v2","worker_name":"asr-worker-v2","protocol":"http",
188:        "endpoint":"http://asr-worker:8002/infer","canary_percent":20}'
189: ```
190: 
191: Send traffic to `/v1/infer/asr`. About 20% of jobs are routed to v2 and never complete. To keep the demo short, run the guard with a lower threshold: `--min-requests 10` needs stable completions of at least `10 * 80 / 20 = 40` inside the window. Run the guard: expect `rollback` with a "canary starved" reason. Re-run with `--apply`: `canary_percent` becomes 0. Run once more: `noop`.
192: 
193: **Demo B: error spike (synthetic).** Insert synthetic `error` rows for a version into ClickHouse (`INSERT INTO request_metrics ...`) so that the canary shows a high error rate against a healthy stable one, run the guard once, and confirm `rollback`. State clearly in the report that these rows were synthetic.
194: 
195: Clean up afterwards: set `canary_percent` to 0, disable the `asr` v2 row through the existing `DELETE /v1/models/{id}` endpoint (this is demo cleanup by a human operator, not part of the guard), and delete the synthetic rows.
196: 
197: ## Task 11.5 (optional): Kubernetes CronJob
198: 
199: Only if the human wants it. Add `k8s/gateway/canary-guard-cronjob.yaml`:
200: 
201: - `schedule: "* * * * *"`, `concurrencyPolicy: Forbid`, `successfulJobsHistoryLimit: 1`, `failedJobsHistoryLimit: 3`.
202: - The gateway image (`imagePullPolicy` as the other manifests use), `command: ["python", "canary_guard.py", "--apply"]`.
203: - Environment: `GATEWAY_URL=http://gateway:8000`, `CLICKHOUSE_HOST=clickhouse`, and `ADMIN_KEY` from a **new Secret** `canary-guard-admin-key`. Create the Secret with `kubectl create secret generic ... --from-literal=...` at deploy time; **do not commit the key**, and do not add a Secret manifest containing a real key.
204: - Apply to a cluster only if one is available; otherwise validate with `kubectl apply --dry-run=client -f` and mark the live run `NOT RUN`.

  1: # Phase 12: ClickHouse Init in Kubernetes and Metrics Pass-Through in Compose
  2: 
  3: > **Historical implementation prompt.** Phase 12 was completed. The exit checks below are retained as the original acceptance criteria; live Minikube evidence and the refreshed smoke baseline are recorded in this report.
  4: 
  5: > Paste this whole file into your coding assistant, inside the ModelMesh repo.
  6: > **Precondition:** Phases 6 to 11 and the review follow-up are merged, `git status` is clean, and `pytest -q` is green (record the passed count; the last recorded run was 102 passed).
  7: > Global rules, the shell rule (Ubuntu WSL terminal) and the report format are in `00_Run_The_Project_Prompt.md` (it lives in `docs/` in the repo), Section A. They apply here.
  8: 
  9: **Goal:** close two small gaps found in the Round 2 review.
 10: 
 11: 1. **Kubernetes (review item N3):** the ClickHouse deployment has no volume and no init script, so after any pod restart the `request_metrics` table is gone, metrics silently stop, and someone must re-run the DDL by hand.
 12: 2. **Compose (review item N2):** `docker-compose.yml` does not pass `CLICKHOUSE_HOST` to the workers, and metrics are off when it is unset, so metrics cannot be switched on under Compose without editing the file.
 13: 
 14: **Effort:** about half a day. **Adds capability:** no. Both tasks make existing metrics easier to run correctly.
 15: 
 16: **Scope fence:** only `k8s/clickhouse/`, `docker-compose.yml`, and the docs listed in Task 12.3. Do **not** touch `metrics.py`, either worker, the canary guard, the gateway, the Postgres manifests, or `docker-compose.test.yml`. Do **not** add a persistent volume for ClickHouse: keeping metrics across restarts is a separate decision for the human. Default Compose behaviour must not change: with no variable set, metrics stay **off**.
 17: 
 18: **Branch:** `phase-12-clickhouse-init`
 19: 
 20: ### Intended behaviour changes (everything else must stay identical)
 21: 
 22: 1. **Kubernetes:** the ClickHouse pod creates the `request_metrics` table by itself at start-up (the table is still empty after a restart, because there is no volume).
 23: 2. **Compose:** both workers accept `CLICKHOUSE_HOST` and `CLICKHOUSE_PORT` from the shell. With nothing set, behaviour is exactly as today (metrics disabled, no connection attempts).
 24: 
 25: Run `scripts/smoke_test.sh` before and after. The `CHECK` lines must be identical. One exception: the `bad-upload` INFO line already differs from `docs/baseline-smoke.txt` because of Phases 9 and 10, so compare it with your "before" run from this phase instead.
 26: 
 27: ---
 28: 
 29: ## Task 12.0: Before-run
 30: 
 31: On a clean branch, with the normal Compose stack up (`docker compose up -d`, then `docker compose ps`):
 32: 
 33: ```bash
 34: scripts/smoke_test.sh > /tmp/smoke-before-phase12.txt
 35: tail -3 /tmp/smoke-before-phase12.txt     # expect: SMOKE: PASS
 36: ```
 37: 
 38: If it does not pass, **stop and report**; do not start the phase on a failing baseline.
 39: 
 40: ## Task 12.1: Create the table automatically when ClickHouse starts
 41: 
 42: The official ClickHouse image runs `*.sql` files found in `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/` when it starts. `clickhouse/init.sql` already uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, so running it on every start is safe.
 43: 
 44: 1. Generate the ConfigMap from the SQL file, never by hand, so the two cannot drift (named `configmap.yaml` so a directory apply creates it before the Deployment):
 45: 
 46: ```bash
 47: kubectl create configmap clickhouse-init-sql --from-file=init.sql=clickhouse/init.sql \
 48:   -n modelmesh --dry-run=client -o yaml > k8s/clickhouse/configmap.yaml
 49: ```
 50: 
 51: This needs `kubectl` but not a running cluster.
 52: 
 53: 2. Edit `k8s/clickhouse/deployment.yaml`. Keep everything that is there (image `clickhouse/clickhouse-server:24.8-alpine`, `CLICKHOUSE_SKIP_USER_SETUP`, ports, resources). Add the mount to the container and the volume to the pod spec:
 54: 
 55: ```yaml
 56:           volumeMounts:
 57:             - name: init-sql
 58:               mountPath: /docker-entrypoint-initdb.d
 59:               readOnly: true
 60:       volumes:
 61:         - name: init-sql
 62:           configMap:
 63:             name: clickhouse-init-sql
 64: ```
 65: 
 66: `volumeMounts` goes under the `clickhouse` container; `volumes` goes at the same level as `containers`.
 67: 
 68: 3. **Do not** add a PersistentVolumeClaim, probes, or change resource limits in this phase.
 69: 
 70: Commit: `phase12: clickhouse creates request_metrics at startup`.
 71: 
 72: ## Task 12.2: Let Compose switch worker metrics on from the shell
 73: 
 74: In `docker-compose.yml`, add these two lines to the `environment:` of **both** worker services (`doc-ocr-worker` and `asr-worker`), next to the other pass-through variables:
 75: 
 76: ```yaml
 77: CLICKHOUSE_HOST: ${CLICKHOUSE_HOST:-}
 78: CLICKHOUSE_PORT: ${CLICKHOUSE_PORT:-8123}
 79: ```
 80: 
 81: An empty `CLICKHOUSE_HOST` is treated the same as unset by the workers (`if not os.getenv("CLICKHOUSE_HOST"): return`), so default behaviour does not change. Keep the file's existing indentation style (check whether `environment:` is a mapping or a list in this file, and match it).

[MD]  docs\archive\Phase2-Async-Job-Queue.md
================================================================================
  1: # Phase 2 — Async Job Queue
  2: 
  3: ## Implementation Status: COMPLETE ✓
  4: 
  5: **Goal:** Decouple gateway from workers using job queues. Gateway returns job_id immediately, workers process asynchronously.
  6: 
  7: **Actual time:** Completed
  8: 
  9: ---
 10: 
 11: ## What Was Implemented
 12: 
 13: ### Core Features (All Complete)
 14: 
 15: - Redis Streams job queue with consumer groups
 16: - Dormant Kafka alternative code (not wired into the application or Compose)
 17: - Async `/v1/infer/{model_name}` endpoint (returns job_id)
 18: - `/v1/jobs/{job_id}` polling endpoint
 19: - Workers filter by `worker_name` from registry
 20: - Retry logic: Max 3 retries, exponential backoff (2s, 4s, 8s)
 21: - Dead-letter queue (`inference_jobs_dlq`)
 22: - Consumer lag monitoring (XLEN, XPENDING)
 23: - Job TTLs (1h status, 2h results)
 24: - Horizontal worker scaling
 25: 
 26: ### Architecture
 27: 
 28: ```
 29: Client → Gateway → Redis Streams → Workers (async)
 30:          ↓                                    ↓
 31:          Returns job_id                       Store result in Redis
 32: 
 33: Client polls /v1/jobs/{job_id} for result
 34: ```
 35: 
 36: ---
 37: 
 38: ## Definition of Done
 39: 
 40: - [x] Gateway publishes jobs to Redis Streams
 41: - [x] Job payload includes model_id, model_name, model_version, worker_name
 42: - [x] Workers filter by worker_name (not hardcoded strings)
 43: - [x] `/v1/jobs/{job_id}` returns real status
 44: - [x] Retry logic with bounded retries + DLQ
 45: - [x] Consumer lag monitoring logged
 46: - [x] Stale model behavior documented
 47: - [x] Kafka alternative code exists but is dormant and not wired/deployed
 48: - [x] Redis Streams is the queue used by the application; Kafka code is dormant
 49: - [x] Payloads are stored in separate Redis keys with a configurable TTL
 50: - [x] Stale pending entries are reclaimed using the worker hostname as part of the consumer name
 51: - [x] Poison messages are a known limit: reclaimed forever until a future delivery-count DLQ policy is added
 52: 
 53: ---
 54: 
 55: ## Key Design Decisions
 56: 
 57: **Why async queue?**
 58: 
 59: - Gateway never blocks on slow inference
 60: - Workers scale independently
 61: - Retries for transient failures; permanent input errors fail without retries
 62: - Better fault tolerance
 63: 
 64: **Trade-off**: REST clients must poll for queued-job results. Phase 3 added a separate direct-inference WebSocket path; it does not turn the REST queue into a streaming job API.
 65: 
 66: **Stale model behavior**: Jobs enqueued before model disabled will still process. Disabling stops NEW jobs, not in-flight ones.
 67: 
 68: **Queue selection**: Redis Streams is wired into the application. Kafka-related code is present but is not wired or deployed.
 69: 
 70: **Crash recovery**: stale pending entries are reclaimed after `RECLAIM_IDLE_MS` (default 300000 ms). A poison message that causes a worker to crash on every delivery is reclaimed forever; delivery-count limits are intentionally not part of this baseline.
 71: 
 72: **Deploy order**: deploy workers first because they accept both legacy payload fields and payload keys; wait for the queue to drain; then deploy the gateway. Rolling the gateway back is safe.
 73: 
 74: ---
 75: 
 76: ## Files Implemented
 77: 
 78: - `gateway/job_queue.py` - Redis Streams queue
 79: - `gateway/kafka_queue.py` - Dormant Kafka alternative code
 80: - `workers/doc_ocr/consumer.py` - Redis consumer with retry logic
 81: - `workers/asr/consumer.py` - Redis consumer
 82: - `workers/doc_ocr/kafka_consumer.py` - Dormant Kafka consumer
 83: - `workers/asr/kafka_consumer.py` - Dormant Kafka consumer
 84: 
 85: ---
 86: 
 87: ## Next Phase
 88: 
 89: Phase 3: WebSocket direct inference + gRPC (a separate whole-file request/result path; transport performance depends on the workload)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  1: # Phase 3 — WebSocket Inference + gRPC
  2: 
  3: ## Implementation Status: COMPLETE ✓
  4: 
  5: **Goal:** WebSocket inference via HTTP or gRPC, with protobuf-based gRPC workers.
  6: 
  7: **Actual time:** Completed
  8: 
  9: ---
 10: 
 11: ## What Was Implemented
 12: 
 13: ### Part A: WebSocket Inference (Client-Facing) - COMPLETE
 14: 
 15: - WebSocket endpoint `/v1/ws/infer/{model_name}`
 16: - API key auth via query parameter (browser WebSocket limitation)
 17: - Whole-file inference responses over WebSocket; the current client flow is not incremental audio upload
 18: - Test clients (Python CLI + browser HTML)
 19: 
 20: ### Part B: gRPC (WebSocket Inference Communication) - COMPLETE
 21: 
 22: - Protobuf contract (`inference.proto`)
 23: - Unary RPC (`Predict`) for direct inference calls
 24: - Bidirectional ASR RPC (`PredictStream`); the current gateway sends the uploaded file as a single final chunk
 25: - gRPC servers in all workers
 26: - gRPC client in gateway
 27: - Protocol selection via registry on the WebSocket inference path (HTTP ↔ gRPC)
 28: 
 29: ### Architecture
 30: 
 31: ```
 32: Client → Gateway (WebSocket) → Worker (HTTP or gRPC) → Result
 33:          Direct inference path; protocol is read from the registry
 34: 
 35: Client → Gateway (REST) → Redis Streams → Worker consumer → Result in Redis
 36:          Async job path; it does not dispatch on the registry protocol
 37: ```
 38: 
 39: ---
 40: 
 41: ## Definition of Done
 42: 
 43: - [x] WebSocket endpoint returns inference results
 44: - [x] Test clients (Python + HTML) working
 45: - [x] `.proto` contract defined
 46: - [x] WebSocket registry rows can select HTTP or gRPC without gateway code changes
 47: - [x] Gateway-worker gRPC calls on the WebSocket path
 48: - [x] Bidirectional streaming gRPC for ASR; the current gateway path sends one final chunk, not token-level streaming
 49: - [x] Before/after latency comparison recorded
 50: - [x] Can explain whether gRPC helped and why
 51: 
 52: ---
 53: 
 54: ## Key Design Decisions
 55: 
 56: **Why WebSockets?**
 57: 
 58: - Avoids REST job-status polling for this direct inference path
 59: - Uses a full-duplex connection, but the current API sends one whole file and returns one final result
 60: - Does not provide incremental upload, transcription tokens, or progress updates
 61: 
 62: **Trade-off**: More complex client lifecycle management. The current gateway receives the full file before forwarding it; it does not provide incremental client-to-worker streaming.
 63: 
 64: **Why gRPC?**
 65: 
 66: - Binary protocol (faster than JSON)
 67: - Strongly-typed contracts (protobuf)
 68: - Built-in streaming
 69: - Lower latency for internal calls
 70: 
 71: **Trade-off**: More complex setup (protobuf compilation)
 72: 
 73: **Protocol Abstraction**: The WebSocket inference path reads protocol and endpoint from the registry. Switching HTTP → gRPC there is a data change:
 74: 
 75: ```sql
 76: UPDATE models SET protocol = 'grpc', endpoint = 'doc-ocr-worker:50051' WHERE name = 'doc-ocr';
 77: ```
 78: 
 79: Zero gateway code changes required.
 80: 
 81: The REST `/v1/infer/{model_name}` path enqueues to Redis Streams, and worker consumers call their inference functions directly. It does not read the model protocol field or route that request through gRPC.
 82: 
 83: **Streaming caveat**: Whisper doesn't support token-level streaming. The ASR gRPC RPC can yield outputs for chunks, but the current gateway sends a whole uploaded file as one chunk with `is_final: true`. This is not incremental client streaming or token-by-token transcription.
 84: 
 85: ---
 86: 
 87: ## Files Implemented
 88: 
 89: - `inference.proto` - Protobuf contract
 90: - `gateway/grpc_client.py` - gRPC client
 91: - `workers/doc_ocr/grpc_server.py` - Unary Predict RPC
 92: - `workers/asr/grpc_server.py` - Predict + PredictStream RPCs
 93: - `workers/*/entrypoint.sh` - Run HTTP + gRPC + Redis consumer concurrently
 94: - `gateway/main.py` - WebSocket endpoint + gRPC routing
 95: - `test_ws_client.py` - Python WebSocket test client
 96: - `test_ws_client.html` - Browser WebSocket test client
 97: - Updated Dockerfiles for protobuf compilation
 98: - Updated docker-compose.yml for gRPC ports
 99: 
100: ---
101: 
102: ## Multi-Service Containers
103: 
104: Workers start THREE separate processes in one container:
105: 
106: 1. HTTP server (Phase 1 legacy, port 8001)
107: 2. gRPC server (Phase 3, port 50051)
108: 3. Redis consumer (Phase 2, foreground)
109: 
110: Achieved via entrypoint script supervision of HTTP and gRPC servers and the Redis consumer. These processes do not share in-memory model instances; each process can load its own model. Final Phase 9 Compose measurements: ASR 1.084 GiB and doc-ocr 1.185 GiB. A later local Minikube OCR sample measured a 2,503 MiB container peak; the Kubernetes doc-ocr limit is 3 GiB. This was a small synthetic sample, not large-document stress testing.
111: 
112: ---
113: 
114: ## Testing
115: 
116: ### WebSocket Test
117: 
118: ```bash
119: python test_ws_client.py <api_key> asr test-audio.wav
120: ```
121: 
122: Browser: Open `test_ws_client.html`, enter API key, select file, click "Connect & Send"
123: 
124: ### Switch Protocol via Registry
125: 
126: ```sql
127: -- HTTP
128: UPDATE models SET protocol = 'http', endpoint = 'http://doc-ocr-worker:8001/infer' WHERE name = 'doc-ocr';
129: 
130: -- gRPC
131: UPDATE models SET protocol = 'grpc', endpoint = 'doc-ocr-worker:50051' WHERE name = 'doc-ocr';
132: ```
133: 
134: No code changes, no restarts. Just data.
135: 
136: ---
137: 
138: ## Performance Notes
139: 
140: Two different comparisons are recorded in [`baseline-measurements.md`](baseline-measurements.md). The Artillery run measured REST job-acceptance latency and could not compare HTTP with gRPC because REST jobs do not use protocol dispatch. A separate custom script timed 20 sequential whole-file WebSocket round trips per protocol and model. Those results are historical measurements, not a performance guarantee; the WebSocket client flow is not incremental streaming.

 71: 
 72: ## Environment Constraints
 73: 
 74: **Minikube setup**: The live follow-up used `--cpus=2 --memory=4096` and stopped Compose first to free host memory. Despite that startup flag, Kubernetes reported 7.58 GiB allocatable, so the run does not establish a hard 4 GiB node-memory limit. The OCR sample, memory peak, and limits are recorded in the [Phase 9 completion report](Phase9_Completion_Report.md).
 75: 
 76: **Image building**: Must run `eval $(minikube docker-env)` before building images in each new terminal session, otherwise images build to host Docker daemon and pods fail to find them.
 77: 
 78: ---
 79: 
 80: ## Quick Start
 81: 
 82: ```bash
 83: # 1. Start minikube
 84: minikube start --cpus=2 --memory=4096
 85: minikube addons enable metrics-server
 86: 
 87: # 2. Build images in minikube's Docker
 88: eval $(minikube docker-env)
 89: docker build -t modelmesh-gateway:latest -f gateway/Dockerfile .
 90: docker build -t modelmesh-doc-ocr:latest -f workers/doc_ocr/Dockerfile .
 91: docker build -t modelmesh-asr:latest -f workers/asr/Dockerfile .
 92: 
 93: # 3. Deploy to Kubernetes
 94: kubectl apply -f k8s/namespace.yaml
 95: kubectl apply -f k8s/secrets.yaml
 96: kubectl apply -f k8s/redis/
 97: kubectl apply -f k8s/postgres/
 98: kubectl apply -f k8s/gateway/
 99: kubectl apply -f k8s/clickhouse/
100: kubectl wait --for=condition=available deployment/clickhouse -n modelmesh --timeout=180s
101: kubectl apply -f k8s/workers/
102: ```
103: 
104: The ClickHouse deployment mounts `k8s/clickhouse/configmap.yaml` at `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d`, so it creates `request_metrics` automatically at startup. The manual command below is a fallback if automatic initialization needs to be rechecked:
105: 
106: ```bash
107: kubectl exec -i -n modelmesh deployment/clickhouse -- clickhouse-client --multiquery < clickhouse/init.sql
108: 
109: # 4. Check status
110: kubectl get pods -n modelmesh
111: kubectl get svc -n modelmesh
112: 
113: # 5. Access gateway
114: kubectl port-forward -n modelmesh svc/gateway 8000:8000
115: curl http://localhost:8000/v1/models
116: ```
117: 
118: `k8s/secrets.yaml` defines the database Secret; `k8s/gateway/jwt-secret.yaml` defines the gateway JWT Secret and is applied by the gateway directory command above. The gateway Service exposes port 8000.
119: 
120: ---
121: 
122: ## Key Design Decisions
123: 
124: **StatefulSet for Postgres**: Stable network identity (postgres-0) + persistent volume survives pod restarts.
125: 
126: **Deployments for workers**: Stateless (job queue handles state), can scale horizontally, rolling updates safe.
127: 
128: **Multi-service containers**: Workers run HTTP (8001) + gRPC (50051) + Redis consumer concurrently. Entrypoint script backgrounds HTTP and gRPC, runs consumer in foreground.
129: 
130: **Resource limits**: All services have requests (minimum guaranteed) and limits (maximum allowed) to prevent resource starvation.
131: 
132: **Secrets management**: Current secrets are committed (NOT for production). Production should use external secret management (AWS Secrets Manager, Vault, etc.).
133: 
134: ---
135: 
136: ## Scaling Workers
137: 
138: ```bash
139: # Scale doc-ocr to 3 replicas
140: kubectl scale deployment doc-ocr-worker -n modelmesh --replicas=3
141: 
142: # Scale ASR to 2 replicas
143: kubectl scale deployment asr-worker -n modelmesh --replicas=2
144: 
145: # Jobs automatically distributed via Redis Streams consumer groups
146: ```
147: 
148: ---
149: 
150: ## Monitoring
151: 
152: ```bash
153: # Pod status
154: kubectl get pods -n modelmesh -w
155: 
156: # Pod logs
157: kubectl logs -n modelmesh -f deployment/gateway
158: kubectl logs -n modelmesh -f deployment/doc-ocr-worker
159: 
160: # Resource usage (requires metrics-server)
161: kubectl top pods -n modelmesh
162: kubectl top nodes
163: ```
164: 
165: ---
166: 
167: ## Health Checks
168: 
169: **Configured gateway readiness probe**: `GET /ready`.
170: 
171: **Configured gateway liveness probe**: `GET /health`.
172: 
173: Both worker Deployments define `/health` readiness and liveness probes. The entrypoint supervises HTTP, gRPC, and Redis consumer processes and exits non-zero if a child process dies.
174: 
175: Failed probes trigger Kubernetes pod restarts.
176: 
177: ---
178: 
179: ## Persistent Storage
180: 
181: **Postgres**: Uses PersistentVolumeClaim (1Gi)
182: 
183: - minikube: hostPath provisioner (data survives pod restarts, not cluster deletion)
184: - Production: Use cloud storage classes (EBS, pd-ssd, managed-premium)
185: 
186: ---
187: 
188: ## Implementation Notes
189: 
190: **Postgres init**: Originally planned to seed schema at container startup. Implemented via ConfigMap mounting `init.sql` into `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/`.
191: 
192: **gRPC ports**: Both Kubernetes worker Services expose gRPC on port 50051. Docker Compose maps the ASR container's internal 50051 to host port 50052:
193: 
194: - doc-ocr-worker: 50051
195: - asr-worker: 50051 (Compose host mapping: 50052)
196: 
197: **Protobuf compilation**: Stubs generated at container startup via entrypoint script, not at build time. Enables iterative development.
