175: | Gateway cannot import `inference_pb2`                                | The protobuf stubs were not generated. They are built at image build time (gateway) and container start (workers) from `inference.proto`                                                       |
176: | Tests leave rows behind                                              | Expected; use a scratch database ([Testing](testing.md#test-hygiene))                                                                                                                          |
177: | Data disappeared after `docker compose down -v`                      | `-v` deletes the Postgres and Redis volumes. Recreate users, keys and models                                                                                                                   |
178: | Redis restart lost jobs on Kubernetes                                | Redis has no volume there; this is expected                                                                                                                                                    |
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\queue-and-workers.md
================================================================================
  1: # Queue and workers
  2: 
  3: How a REST job travels from the gateway to a worker and back, what happens when something fails, and what each worker actually does. Storage details (key names, TTLs) are in [Data stores](data-stores.md).
  4: 
  5: ## Job lifecycle
  6: 
  7: ```mermaid
  8: stateDiagram-v2
  9:     [*] --> queued: gateway enqueues
 10:     queued --> processing: worker picks it up
 11:     processing --> success: inference returned
 12:     processing --> processing: transient error, retry (up to 3)
 13:     processing --> failed: permanent error, or retries exhausted
 14:     queued --> expired: no consumer, or payload TTL passed
 15:     success --> [*]
 16:     failed --> [*]
 17:     expired --> [*]
 18: ```
 19: 
 20: | Status       | Visible to the client as                                  | Notes                                                               |
 21: | ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 22: | `queued`     | `{ job_id, status: "queued", model_name, model_version }` | Written by the gateway                                              |
 23: | `processing` | `status: "processing"`                                    | Stays `processing` during retries; it is never set back to `queued` |
 24: | `success`    | `{ job_id, status: "success", result: { … } }`            | Result lives 2 hours                                                |
 25: | `failed`     | `{ job_id, status: "failed", error: "<real message>" }`   | The error is the actual exception text, not a generic message       |
 26: | expired      | HTTP `404 Job not found or expired`                       | Also returned for a job that belongs to another user                |
 27: 
 28: Real result shapes (captured by running the consumer logic against Redis with stubbed inference):
 29: 
 30: ```json
 31: { "job_id": "344b70d6-…", "status": "success", "result": { "text": "hi", "avg_confidence": 0.93, "line_count": 1 } }
 32: { "job_id": "6857a33c-…", "status": "failed",  "error": "Invalid image data" }
 33: ```
 34: 
 35: ## Consumer algorithm
 36: 
 37: Each worker container runs one consumer process. It reads **one entry at a time** (`XREADGROUP … COUNT 1 BLOCK 5000`) from the shared stream `inference_jobs`, using the consumer group `{WORKER_NAME}_group` and the consumer name `{WORKER_NAME}-{HOSTNAME}`.
 38: 
 39: ```mermaid
 40: flowchart TD
 41:     A["Entry read from inference_jobs"] --> B{"worker_name equals<br/>this worker?"}
 42:     B -->|"no"| X["XACK and skip"]
 43:     B -->|"yes"| C["Set status processing"]
 44:     C --> D{"Payload key present?"}
 45:     D -->|"no"| P["PermanentError: payload expired"]
 46:     D -->|"yes"| E["Run inference"]
 47:     E -->|"ValueError"| P
 48:     E -->|"any other exception"| T["Transient failure"]
 49:     E -->|"ok"| S["Write result, delete status key,<br/>metric success, XACK"]
 50:     T --> R{"retry_count below 3?"}
 51:     R -->|"yes"| W["Sleep 2, 4 or 8 s inside the loop,<br/>XADD a NEW entry with retry_count + 1, XACK the old one"]
 52:     R -->|"no"| F
 53:     P --> F["Status failed with the real error, result key,<br/>DLQ entry, metric error, XACK"]
 54: ```
 55: 
 56: ### Failure handling
 57: 
 58: | Case                                                                          | What happens                                                                                                                                                                | Verified                                            |
 59: | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
 60: | Entry for another worker                                                      | Acknowledged and skipped. **Every** worker's group sees every entry                                                                                                         | Code                                                |
 61: | Invalid input (`ValueError` from inference, for example "Invalid image data") | **Permanent**: no retry; status `failed` with that message; one DLQ entry; one `error` metric                                                                               | Run: 1 attempt, no sleeps, 1 DLQ entry              |
 62: | Payload key missing (expired)                                                 | Permanent: `payload expired`                                                                                                                                                | Code                                                |
 63: | Any other exception                                                           | **Transient**: up to 3 retries with sleeps of 2, 4 and 8 s, so **4 attempts** in total                                                                                      | Run: 4 attempts, sleeps `[2, 4, 8]`                 |
 64: | Retries exhausted                                                             | Status `failed` with the real last error; one DLQ entry; **one** `error` metric (retries write none)                                                                        | Run                                                 |
 65: | Worker crashes mid-job                                                        | The entry stays pending; after `RECLAIM_IDLE_MS` (default 300,000 ms) any consumer in the group reclaims it with `XAUTOCLAIM` and reprocesses it                            | Test with a real Redis; recorded live on Kubernetes |
 66: | Crash after writing the result but before `XACK`                              | Redelivered later and processed again (at-least-once): the result is overwritten, a duplicate metric row is possible                                                        | Reasoning from the code                             |
 67: | An entry that crashes the worker every time                                   | Reclaimed again and again; there is no delivery-count limit                                                                                                                 | Documented limitation                               |
 68: | Job enqueued, then the model is disabled                                      | Still processed. The consumer never consults the registry                                                                                                                   | Documented in Phase 2                               |
 69: | Registered `worker_name` has **no worker running**                            | The job is acknowledged-and-skipped by every other group and **never processed**. No error, no DLQ entry; the client sees `queued` until the status key expires, then `404` | Derived from the code and the recorded canary demo  |
 70: | Redis unavailable                                                             | Consumer logs the error, sleeps 1 s and retries the loop; the gateway returns 500 for enqueue                                                                               | Code                                                |
 71: 
 72: A retry adds a **new** stream entry and acknowledges the old one. One job that fails after all retries therefore leaves four entries in the stream (all acknowledged).
 73: 
 74: ### Head-of-line blocking
 75: 
 76: The backoff `sleep` runs **inside the consumer loop**, which handles one entry at a time. A job that fails transiently blocks its consumer for up to 2 + 4 + 8 = **14 seconds** of sleeping before the final failure, and other jobs wait. Scale replicas, not threads.
 77: 
 78: ### Maintenance
 79: 
 80: At most every 30 seconds (checked after each read, which returns within 5 s) the consumer:
 81: 
 82: 1. logs `Stream length: N, Pending: M` and warns if the stream is above 80% of `STREAM_MAXLEN`;
 83: 2. reclaims stale pending entries with `XAUTOCLAIM` (batches of 10, up to 100 batches, so at most 1,000 entries per pass).
 84: 
 85: Reclaim also runs once at start-up. `RECLAIM_IDLE_MS` must exceed your slowest job plus the 14 s of backoff, or a healthy job can be reclaimed while it is still running.
 86: 
 87: ## The workers
 88: 
 89: Both workers expose the same three interfaces and share the same consumer and metrics code. They differ only in the model.
 90: 
 91: |                                 | `doc-ocr`                                                                           | `asr`                                                                                    |
 92: | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 93: | Model                           | EasyOCR, English only, CPU                                                          | OpenAI Whisper `tiny` (`WHISPER_MODEL`), CPU                                             |
 94: | Input                           | Image bytes (decoded with OpenCV)                                                   | Audio bytes (decoded by `ffmpeg` through Whisper)                                        |
 95: | Result                          | `{ "text": "<lines joined by \n>", "avg_confidence": 0.0 to 1.0, "line_count": n }` | `{ "text": "…" }` only (no confidence, segments or language)                             |
 96: | Invalid input                   | `ValueError("Invalid image data")`                                                  | `RuntimeError` "Failed to load audio" is converted to `ValueError("Invalid audio data")` |
 97: | HTTP port                       | 8001                                                                                | 8002                                                                                     |
 98: | gRPC                            | `Predict` only                                                                      | `Predict` and `PredictStream`                                                            |
 99: | Registry seed                   | `supports_streaming = false`                                                        | `supports_streaming = true`                                                              |
100: | Model loading                   | At **import** of `inference.py`                                                     | Lazily, under a lock, preloaded at start-up                                              |
101: | Reported memory (idle, Compose) | 1.185 GiB                                                                           | 1.084 GiB                                                                                |
102: 
103: Neither worker limits image dimensions or audio duration; only the byte cap applies. A small compressed upload can still decode to something large.
104: 
105: ### HTTP interface
106: 
107: `POST /infer` with a multipart `file`. It reads the whole file and returns JSON; invalid input is `400`. The ASR worker also checks the content type against an allow-list (audio types and `application/octet-stream`, which is what the gateway sends). `GET /health` always returns `{"status": "ok"}`: it does not check the model, Redis or ClickHouse.
108: 
109: The workers do not enforce a size limit; the gateway does.
110: 
111: ### gRPC servers
112: 
113: The service is `InferenceWorker`. The `.proto` file was not in the reviewed export, so this is the contract **inferred from how the code uses it**:
114: 
115: | RPC                                       | Request                                                                    | Response                                                                                |
116: | ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
117: | `Predict` (unary)                         | `InferenceRequest { payload: bytes, model_name: string }`                  | `InferenceResponse { result_json: string, latency_ms: float }`                          |
118: | `PredictStream` (bidirectional, ASR only) | stream of `InferenceChunk { chunk: bytes, sequence: int, is_final: bool }` | stream of `InferenceResult { partial_text: string, is_final: bool, confidence: float }` |
119: 
120: Behaviour to be aware of:
121: 
122: - **`PredictStream` transcribes each chunk independently** as a complete audio file. It is not incremental transcription. The gateway sends exactly **one** final chunk, so in practice it is whole-file transcription.
123: - **`confidence` is always `0.0`**, because `transcribe()` returns only text.
124: - A per-chunk failure is returned as a normal result whose `partial_text` is `[Error: <message>]`, indistinguishable from a transcript except by that prefix.
125: - `Predict` reports failures with gRPC status `INTERNAL` and the raw exception text in the details.
126: - The OCR worker has no `PredictStream`; calling it returns `UNIMPLEMENTED`.
127: - Insecure channels, no TLS. The gateway opens a **new channel per call** (30 s timeout for `Predict`, 60 s for `PredictStream`). Message limit is `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES + 1 MiB` on both sides.
128: - Server threads: `GRPC_MAX_WORKERS` (10 locally, 3 in the Kubernetes manifests). Concurrent calls share one model instance without a lock around inference.
129: 
130: ### Supervision
131: 
132: `entrypoint.sh` compiles the protobuf stubs, then starts the HTTP server, the gRPC server and the consumer in the background. If **any** exits, it stops the others and exits with code 1, so the container restarts (`restart: on-failure` in Compose; the Kubernetes container restart policy). On `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT` it stops the children and exits 0. Kubernetes also probes `/health` on each worker.
133: 
134: ## Throughput and scaling
135: 
136: - One consumer process handles one job at a time; replicas of the same worker share the consumer group, so each entry goes to one of them.
137: - `kubectl scale deployment doc-ocr-worker --replicas=3` is the documented way to add capacity. Each replica loads its own three model copies.
138: - Compose runs one container per worker.
139: - No autoscaling exists.
140: 
141: ## Metrics
142: 
143: Each worker writes one ClickHouse row per finished job when `CLICKHOUSE_HOST` is set. See [Data stores](data-stores.md#clickhouse).
144: 
145: ## Kafka (dormant)
146: 
147: `gateway/kafka_queue.py` and `workers/*/kafka_consumer.py` are not imported anywhere and not deployed. They have drifted from the Redis path: no retries, no dead-letter queue, no permanent/transient split, no reclaim, no metrics, no owner key, and a different enqueue signature. The worker's Kafka module hard-codes its worker name. They are kept and labelled dormant, not maintained. `aiokafka` is still installed in the gateway and worker images.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\registry-and-canary.md
================================================================================
  1: # Registry and canary
  2: 
  3: How models are registered and chosen, how a canary split works, how to run one, and what the optional rollback guard does. Endpoint details are in [API](api.md).
  4: 
  5: ## The model registry
  6: 
  7: Every model version is one row in the Postgres `models` table (see [Data stores](data-stores.md#postgres)).
  8: 
  9: | Field                  | Meaning                                                                        |
 10: | ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
 11: | `name`                 | What clients ask for (`/v1/infer/{name}`)                                      |
 12: | `version`              | `v<number>`. New registrations must match `^v\d+$`                             |
 13: | `worker_name`          | Which worker consumer group handles **REST jobs** for this version             |
 14: | `protocol`, `endpoint` | How the gateway calls the worker directly. **Used only by the WebSocket path** |
 15: | `status`               | `active` or `disabled` (`deleted` exists in the schema but nothing sets it)    |
 16: | `supports_streaming`   | With `protocol = grpc`, the WebSocket path uses `PredictStream`                |
 17: | `canary_percent`       | 0 to 100; meaningful on the **highest** active version                         |
 18: 
 19: Registering a model records metadata only. **It does not deploy anything.** A worker that consumes `worker_name` must already be running, or REST jobs for that model are never processed (see [failure handling](queue-and-workers.md#failure-handling)).
 20: 
 21: Registry changes take effect on the next request. The gateway reads Postgres on every call and keeps no cache.
 22: 
 23: ## How a model version is chosen (REST)
 24: 
 25: ```mermaid
 26: flowchart TD
 27:     A["POST /v1/infer/{name}"] --> B{"?version= given?"}
 28:     B -->|"yes"| P["Exact lookup of that version.<br/>Canary is bypassed"]
 29:     B -->|"no"| L["List ACTIVE versions of the name"]
 30:     L --> N{"How many?"}
 31:     N -->|"0"| Z{"Does v1 exist?"}
 32:     Z -->|"yes, disabled"| E503["503 Model is currently disabled"]
 33:     Z -->|"no"| E404["404 Unknown model"]
 34:     N -->|"1"| ONE["Use it"]
 35:     N -->|"2 or more"| R["Roll 1 to 100"]
 36:     R -->|"roll at most canary_percent"| CAN["Highest numeric version<br/>(the canary)"]
 37:     R -->|"otherwise"| STB["Lowest numeric version<br/>(the stable)"]
 38:     P --> S{"Status active?"}
 39:     S -->|"no"| E503
 40:     S -->|"yes"| OK["Enqueue for that version's worker_name"]
 41:     ONE --> OK
 42:     CAN --> OK
 43:     STB --> OK
 44: ```
 45: 
 46: Rules, all checked by running the gateway against a real database:
 47: 
 48: - **Highest and lowest are numeric.** `v10` outranks `v9`. With versions `v1`, `v2`, `v9`, `v10` and 30% on the highest, 400 unpinned requests went 275 to `v1` and 125 to `v10`; `v2` and `v9` received **nothing**. Versions in between never get traffic.
 49: - **The percentage is read from the canary row** (the highest version). Setting `canary_percent` on the stable row has no effect.
 50: - **The split is probabilistic.** A 50% split over 20 requests produced 10 and 10 in one recorded run; do not rely on exact ratios for small samples.
 51: - **A single active version is used as-is.** A model with only `v2` active routes to `v2`.
 52: - **Pinning with `?version=` bypasses the canary** and returns `404` for an unknown version or `503` for a disabled one.
 53: - **Quirk:** when no version is active, the gateway checks only for `v1` to decide between 503 and 404. A model whose **only** version is a disabled `v2` returns `404 Unknown model`, not `503`.
 54: - `GET /v1/models/{name}` also defaults to `v1`, so a model with only `v2` needs `?version=v2`.
 55: 
 56: The WebSocket path does **not** use this logic. It defaults to `v1` unless `?version=` is given, and never applies a canary.
 57: 
 58: ## Switching a model's protocol (WebSocket path only)
 59: 
 60: The WebSocket path calls the worker at `endpoint` over `protocol`. Changing them is a registry update:
 61: 
 62: ```bash
 63: curl -X PATCH http://localhost:8000/v1/models/$MODEL_ID \
 64:   -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_KEY" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
 65:   -d '{"protocol": "grpc", "endpoint": "doc-ocr-worker:50051"}'
 66: ```
 67: 
 68: HTTP endpoints are full URLs (`http://doc-ocr-worker:8001/infer`); gRPC endpoints are `host:port` with no scheme. This has **no effect on REST jobs**, which always use `worker_name` and the Redis queue.
 69: 
 70: Recorded comparison (Phase 3, 20 sequential WebSocket requests per protocol; reproducible with `ws_latency_compare.py`): gRPC was about 6% faster at the median for OCR (369.9 ms vs 394.2 ms) and about 3% for ASR (739.5 ms vs 760.4 ms). Inference time dominates; the value of gRPC here is a typed contract, not speed. The script switches the model to gRPC and **does not restore it**, so check the registry afterwards.
 71: 
 72: ## Adding a model or a version
 73: 
 74: 1. **Build a worker** that follows the same contract as the two existing ones: an HTTP `POST /infer` (for WebSocket calls), optionally a gRPC `Predict`, and a consumer. The simplest path is to copy an existing worker directory, change `inference.py`, and set a unique `WORKER_NAME`. The consumer must read `payload:{job_id}` and write `result:{job_id}` in the formats described in [Data stores](data-stores.md#redis).
 75: 2. **Deploy it** (Compose service or Kubernetes Deployment + Service).
 76: 3. **Register it** with an admin key:
 77: 
 78:    ```bash
 79:    curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/v1/models \
 80:      -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_KEY" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
 81:      -d '{"name":"doc-ocr","version":"v2","worker_name":"doc-ocr-worker-v2",
 82:           "protocol":"http","endpoint":"http://doc-ocr-worker-v2:8001/infer",
 83:           "canary_percent":0,"description":"candidate"}'
 84:    ```
 85: 
 86:    Response: `{ "id": "<uuid>", "status": "registered" }`. A duplicate name and version returns `409`.
 87: 
 88: 4. **Send traffic.** Start at `canary_percent` 0 and test with `?version=v2`; raise the percentage when ready.
 89: 
 90: Notes: a brand-new name (not a new version) works the same way, with `v1`. Updating a row cannot change its `name` or `version`, and it cannot clear `description` once set.
 91: 
 92: ## Running a canary
 93: 
 94: | Step      | Action                                                                                                            |
 95: | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 96: | Register  | Add `v2` with a `worker_name` that has a running worker                                                           |
 97: | Start     | `PATCH` `canary_percent` to a small number, for example 10                                                        |
 98: | Watch     | Query ClickHouse for error rate and latency by `model_version` (see [Data stores](data-stores.md#useful-queries)) |
 99: | Promote   | `PATCH` `canary_percent` to 100, then disable `v1`                                                                |
100: | Roll back | `PATCH` `canary_percent` to **0**. Do **not** disable `v2` as part of a rollback                                  |
101: 
102: Metrics come only from the Redis workers and require ClickHouse (Kubernetes, with the DDL applied). With no ClickHouse, you can still split traffic but you cannot measure the result.
103: 
104: Two limits to remember:
105: 
106: - Jobs already assigned to a canary whose worker is down or gone stay unprocessed until their keys expire; clients polling them eventually get `404`.
107: - Pinned requests and WebSocket traffic are not part of the split. Pinned requests are counted in the metrics under the version they asked for.
108: 
109: The recorded canary verification used `v1` and `v2` pointed at the **same** worker. It proves the routing mechanism, not a real rollout.
110: 
111: ## The rollback guard
112: 
113: `gateway/canary_guard.py` is an optional, standalone CLI. It is not part of request handling and is not scheduled anywhere (the optional Kubernetes CronJob was skipped).
114: 
115: For every model with two or more active versions and `canary_percent > 0`, it compares the lowest (stable) and highest (canary) versions over a look-back window using ClickHouse final-outcome rows, and decides.
116: 
117: ```mermaid
118: flowchart TD
119:     A["canary_percent"] -->|"is 0"| N0["noop: canary already at 0"]
120:     A -->|"above 0"| B{"stable completions<br/>below min-requests?"}
121:     B -->|"yes"| H1["hold: not enough stable traffic"]
122:     B -->|"no"| C["expected = stable x pct / max(1, 100 - pct)"]
123:     C --> D{"expected at least min-requests<br/>and canary below 20% of expected?"}
124:     D -->|"yes"| RB1["ROLLBACK: canary starved"]
125:     D -->|"no"| E{"canary completions<br/>below min-requests?"}
126:     E -->|"yes"| H2["hold: not enough canary traffic"]
127:     E -->|"no"| F{"canary error rate minus stable error rate<br/>greater than error-delta?"}
128:     F -->|"yes"| RB2["ROLLBACK: canary error rate"]
129:     F -->|"no"| H3["hold: healthy"]
130: ```
131: 
132: **Why a starvation rule exists.** The queue is asynchronous. If the canary's worker is down, its jobs are never processed and **produce no errors**, so an error-rate rule alone would never fire. The starvation rule notices that the canary completes far fewer jobs than its share implies.
133: 
134: ### Usage
135: 
136: ```bash
137: # Dry run (the default): prints decisions, changes nothing
138: CLICKHOUSE_HOST=localhost GATEWAY_URL=http://localhost:8000 python gateway/canary_guard.py
139: 
140: # Act: PATCHes canary_percent to 0 on a rollback decision, nothing else
141: ADMIN_KEY=... python gateway/canary_guard.py --apply
142: ```
143: 
144: | Option or variable                    | Default                 | Meaning                                                     |
145: | ------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
146: | `--window-min`                        | 10                      | Look-back window in minutes                                 |
147: | `--min-requests`                      | 30                      | Minimum completions per version for the data to count       |
148: | `--error-delta`                       | 0.10                    | Error-rate margin the canary may exceed stable by           |
149: | `--apply`                             | off                     | Perform rollbacks. Requires `ADMIN_KEY` in the environment  |
150: | `GATEWAY_URL`                         | `http://localhost:8000` | Where to read models and send the `PATCH`                   |
151: | `CLICKHOUSE_HOST` / `CLICKHOUSE_PORT` | `clickhouse` / 8123     | Metrics store (the default host name is the Kubernetes one) |
152: 
153: Pass the admin key by environment only, never on the command line. It prints one JSON line per model pair (time, model, versions, percent, per-version counts and error rates, decision, reason, `applied`) and exits 0 unless the guard itself fails. It reads `GET /v1/models` without authentication.
154: 
155: ### What the decision function does (verified by running it)
156: 
157: With the default settings:
158: 
159: | Situation                                                                                | Decision                                                          |
160: | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
161: | Healthy canary **just enabled** at 20%; stable has 200 completions; canary has 0, 3 or 9 | **rollback** ("canary starved: N completed vs about 50 expected") |
162: | Same, canary has 10 or 20                                                                | hold ("not enough canary traffic")                                |
163: | Same, canary has 50                                                                      | hold ("healthy")                                                  |
164: | Stable has 29 completions                                                                | hold ("not enough stable traffic")                                |
165: | Canary error rate 0.30, stable 0.00, enough traffic                                      | rollback                                                          |
166: | Canary error rate exactly 0.10 above stable                                              | hold; 0.11 above → rollback                                       |
167: | `canary_percent` 0                                                                       | noop                                                              |
168: 
169: The starvation rule can only fire when `expected` reaches `--min-requests`. At the default minimum of 30, stable needs this many completions in the window:
170: 
171: | `canary_percent` | Stable completions needed |
172: | ---------------- | ------------------------: |
173: | 5                |                       570 |
174: | 10               |                       270 |
175: | 20               |                       120 |
176: | 50               |                        30 |
177: 
178: So with stable 73 and canary 0 at 20%, the default settings return **hold**, not rollback. The recorded live demo (90 ASR jobs, a rollback with "0 completed vs about 18 expected") must have used a lowered `--min-requests`, as the Phase 11 plan specifies (`--min-requests 10`).
179: 
180: ### Limitations
181: 
182: - **No memory of when the canary started.** The window is a plain look-back, so right after enabling or raising a canary the guard can roll back a healthy canary (the 0, 3, 9 rows above). **Operating rule: after enabling or raising a canary, wait one full window before running with `--apply`, and never schedule `--apply` more often than the window.**
183: - **100% edge case.** At `canary_percent = 100` the formula divides by `max(1, 0) = 1`, so "expected" is the stable completions times 100. With stable at 100 completions still inside the window and a canary that completed 500, the function returns rollback ("500 completed vs about 10000 expected"); it holds only if the canary completed 2,000 or more. Once stable traffic has aged out of the window (fewer than 30 completions) it holds. Wait a window after promoting.
184: - **Pinned traffic distorts the starvation estimate.** The formula assumes every request was routed by the canary split, but pinned requests are counted under their version.
185: - **Metrics are final outcomes from the Redis workers only.** WebSocket traffic is invisible to it.
186: - **Rollback only sets `canary_percent` to 0.** It never disables, promotes or deploys anything, and it has no notifications, statistical testing or latency rule.
187: - The version ordering rule is duplicated from the gateway.
188: - Thin traffic means the guard holds; it never rolls back on too little data.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\security.md
================================================================================
  1: # Security
  2: 
  3: What protects the system today, what does not, and what an operator must add before exposing it. This is a source review plus runtime checks against a real gateway, Postgres and Redis. It is **not** a penetration test, and nothing here has had external review. The project's own README says it is a development implementation and not production-ready; this document agrees and explains why.
  4: 
  5: ## What is being protected
  6: 
  7: Users upload **documents and audio**, which may be sensitive, and get extracted text back. The system also holds credentials (password hashes, API-key hashes, a JWT signing secret) and an internal network of unauthenticated workers.
  8: 
  9: ```mermaid
 10: flowchart LR
 11:     C["Client<br/>(untrusted)"] -->|"plain HTTP and WebSocket,<br/>no TLS"| G["Gateway<br/>auth, limits, registry"]
 12:     G -->|"SQL"| P[("Postgres<br/>users, key hashes, registry")]
 13:     G -->|"payloads, results, limits"| R[("Redis<br/>no password")]
 14:     R --> W["Workers<br/>no authentication"]
 15:     G -.->|"WebSocket path:<br/>direct call, plaintext"| W
 16:     W -.->|"best effort"| CH[("ClickHouse<br/>dev user setup")]
 17: ```
 18: 
 19: Everything to the right of the gateway trusts the network it sits on. There is no authentication, encryption or network policy between internal components.
 20: 
 21: ## Controls that exist
 22: 
 23: | Area                 | Control                                                                                                                                        | Where                     |
 24: | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
 25: | Passwords            | bcrypt hashing; at least 8 characters for new accounts                                                                                         | `auth.py`, `schemas.py`   |
 26: | API keys             | 256-bit random keys; only a SHA-256 hash is stored; a revoked flag is honoured on lookup                                                       | `auth.py`                 |
 27: | Admin                | Registry writes need `is_admin`; admin cannot be granted through the API                                                                       | `auth.py`                 |
 28: | Job privacy          | A job is visible only to the user who created it; a foreign job returns the same `404` as a missing one                                        | `job_queue.py` (verified) |
 29: | Rate limiting        | Atomic per-key counter in Redis                                                                                                                | `rate_limit.py`           |
 30: | Upload size          | One cap (default 10 MiB) on REST and both WebSocket paths; also `--ws-max-size` on the server                                                  | `main.py`, Dockerfile     |
 31: | Registry input       | `version` pattern, `protocol` and `status` literals, `canary_percent` range, enforced by Pydantic and by database CHECK constraints            | `schemas.py`, `init.sql`  |
 32: | SQL                  | Named parameters throughout; the one dynamic `UPDATE` builds its column list from a fixed allow-list                                           | `registry.py`             |
 33: | Public model listing | `endpoint` and `worker_name` are not returned                                                                                                  | `main.py` (verified)      |
 34: | Logging              | `api_key=` query values are redacted in the server's access and error logs                                                                     | `main.py`                 |
 35: | Secrets in config    | Database URL and JWT secret come from environment or Kubernetes Secrets; a warning is logged if the JWT secret is the placeholder              | `auth.py`, manifests      |
 36: | Network exposure     | Compose does not publish Postgres or Redis (only the test override does); Kubernetes uses ClusterIP for everything except the gateway NodePort | Compose, `k8s/`           |
 37: | Revoked keys         | Rejected on the WebSocket path too (it shares the same lookup)                                                                                 | `main.py`                 |
 38: 
 39: ## Gaps
 40: 
 41: Every item was confirmed by reading the code; those marked **verified** were also reproduced.
 42: 
 43: ### Transport and exposure
 44: 
 45: - **No TLS anywhere**, including gRPC, which uses insecure channels. Credentials, uploads and results cross the network in clear text. No Ingress or TLS termination exists in the manifests.
 46: - **Worker endpoints have no authentication.** In Compose, ports 8001, 8002, 50051 and 50052 are **published to the host**, so anyone who can reach the host can call a worker and bypass the gateway's auth and rate limit. In Kubernetes they are cluster-internal.
 47: - **Redis has no password**, and the Kubernetes Redis has no NetworkPolicy in front of it. Anything that can reach it can read uploaded payloads and results.
 48: - `/docs`, `/redoc` and `/openapi.json` are served without authentication (**verified**). The app sets no security headers and no CORS policy.
 49: 
 50: ### Authentication and abuse
 51: 
 52: - **Open registration.** Anyone can create an account and API keys. Registration and login are **not rate limited**; login runs bcrypt on every attempt, so it is both brute-forceable and a CPU lever.
 53: - **The per-key limit is not a per-user limit.** A user chooses `rate_limit_per_min` (1 to 1000) for each key and can create unlimited keys.
 54: - **No key management.** No endpoint lists or revokes keys, and none creates admin keys; both are done in SQL. The `revoked` column is read but never written by the application.
 55: - **A placeholder JWT secret is allowed.** If `JWT_SECRET` is unset, the code logs a warning and runs; the Compose file substitutes `change-me-in-production`. A forged JWT can create API keys for any user ID.
 56: - **API keys travel in the WebSocket URL** (`?api_key=`). The gateway redacts its own logs; reverse proxies and browser history may not.
 57: - **The WebSocket is accepted before authentication.** Unauthenticated clients can hold a connection open briefly before being closed with 4401.
 58: - An admin can set a model's `endpoint` to **any string**. The gateway will POST user uploads to it on the WebSocket path. Admin keys are therefore powerful; treat them like root credentials. (The Phase 11 report records a local admin credential that was accidentally exposed and rotated; the guard script takes the admin key from the environment, not the command line, for this reason.)
 59: 
 60: ### Information disclosure
 61: 
 62: - **WebSocket errors return raw exception text** (**verified**). When a worker responds with an HTTP error, the client receives a message containing the worker's internal URL, which `/v1/models` is built to hide. gRPC failures return the worker's exception text in the status details.
 63: - **`GET /v1/models` is public** and shows model IDs, versions, status and `canary_percent`.
 64: - Worker `/health` is unauthenticated and uninformative by design.
 65: 
 66: ### Data handling
 67: 
 68: - **Uploads are retained after processing.** The payload stays in Redis for an hour even after the job succeeds (**verified**); results and ownership for two hours; both are lost on a Redis restart in Kubernetes. There is no per-job delete endpoint and no encryption at rest.
 69: - Results contain the extracted text or transcript, which may be personal data.
 70: - The size cap on REST is checked **after** the body is parsed; there is no HTTP-level body limit in the application. Put a limit in front of it.
 71: - Memory exhaustion: with the Kubernetes Redis limit (200 MB, no eviction), a single client sending maximum-size uploads makes the **16th request fail with HTTP 500** (**verified**), and `/ready` keeps returning 200. See [Data stores](data-stores.md).
 72: - Neither worker limits image dimensions or audio duration, and both decode untrusted input (OpenCV, EasyOCR, `ffmpeg` through Whisper) as root.
 73: 
 74: ### Deployment hygiene
 75: 
 76: - **Development secrets are committed or inline**: a plaintext Postgres password in Compose, two Kubernetes Secret manifests with placeholder values (not in the reviewed export, described in the setup docs). Replace them.
 77: - **All containers run as root**; no `securityContext`, `runAsNonRoot`, read-only filesystem, resource quotas beyond requests and limits, or NetworkPolicies.
 78: - ClickHouse runs with `CLICKHOUSE_SKIP_USER_SETUP=1` in development (no default-user password).
 79: - No dependency or image scanning, and no CI pipeline.
 80: - A model-registry write by anyone with an admin key takes effect on the next request with no audit trail.
 81: 
 82: ## What an operator must add before real use
 83: 
 84: 1. **TLS** in front of the gateway (Ingress or a reverse proxy), and an internal mesh or at least a NetworkPolicy so only the gateway reaches workers, Redis and Postgres.
 85: 2. A **real `JWT_SECRET`** (and make the gateway refuse to start without it).
 86: 3. **Registration control** (disable, invite-only, or rate limited) and a **per-user** request cap.
 87: 4. A **request body size limit** at the edge.
 88: 5. A **Redis password**, persistence and a memory policy that fails gracefully; alert on memory use because `/ready` will not.
 89: 6. **Replace development secrets**; do not use the committed values.
 90: 7. Do not publish worker ports; remove the host mappings in Compose or bind them to localhost.
 91: 8. Decide **retention**: how long uploads and results may remain, and how to delete them.
 92: 9. Remove or protect `/docs` and `/openapi.json`; stop returning raw exception text over the WebSocket.
 93: 10. Run containers as a non-root user and add resource and image scanning to a pipeline.
 94: 
 95: ## What was verified for this document
 96: 
 97: Against a real Postgres 16 and Redis 7:
 98: 
 99: - Foreign users cannot read a job (404); the owner can.
100: - `/v1/models` entries contain no `endpoint` or `worker_name`.
101: - The WebSocket error path returns the internal worker URL.
102: - A finished job's payload remains in Redis (TTL 3600).
103: - A full Redis causes a 500 on upload while `/ready` stays 200.
104: - `/docs`, `/redoc`, `/openapi.json` return 200; `/metrics` returns 404; a CORS preflight returns 405 and no security headers are present.
105: - No code writes the `requests` table or sets `revoked`.
106: 
107: Not verified: TLS behaviour (none), Kubernetes network behaviour, container hardening, dependency vulnerabilities (no scan was run), and the behaviour of the real EasyOCR and Whisper models on hostile input.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\status-and-limitations.md
================================================================================
  1: # Status and limitations
  2: 
  3: Where the project stands: what was verified, what is known to be wrong or missing, what the design deliberately leaves out, and how it got here. Items marked **verified** were reproduced during this review on a real Postgres and Redis. Items marked **reported** come from the project's own completion reports and were not re-run.
  4: 
  5: ## Verdict
  6: 
  7: **A well-documented development implementation, not production-ready.** The project says so itself, and the evidence agrees.
  8: 
  9: What is solid: the core request path works and is tested (102 tests pass, reproduced twice); the queue has real retry, dead-letter and crash-recovery behaviour with tests behind it; the registry, canary routing and rollback guard behave as described; the documents the project shipped were largely honest about gaps.
 10: 
 11: What stands between it and production:
 12: 
 13: 1. **No transport or network security.** No TLS anywhere, no authentication on workers or Redis, open registration, development secrets in the repository. See [Security](security.md).
 14: 2. **Fragile capacity behaviour.** A full Redis returns bare HTTP 500s while readiness stays green, and uploads linger in Redis for an hour.
 15: 3. **Thin observability.** Metrics need a hand-applied ClickHouse DDL and exist only on Kubernetes; there is no Prometheus, Grafana, alerting or tracing.
 16: 4. **Narrow test coverage of the edges.** No end-to-end test, almost no WebSocket coverage, no `PredictStream` test, nothing that exercises the images, manifests or ClickHouse.
 17: 5. **Evidence is small-sample.** Latency and memory figures come from a handful of requests with tiny fixtures, run by the author.
 18: 
 19: ## What has been verified
 20: 
 21: | Claim                                                                                                                                | Evidence                                                                               | Reproduced here                                                                       |
 22: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 23: | The full test suite passes                                                                                                           | `pytest -q` twice against real Postgres 16 and Redis 7.0.15                            | **Yes**: 102 passed, 8 warnings, both runs                                            |
 24: | The schema is consistent                                                                                                             | `init.sql` plus both migrations applied cleanly; ConfigMap SQL identical to `init.sql` | **Yes**                                                                               |
 25: | Job lifecycle: permanent error, retries, DLQ, one metric                                                                             | Consumer logic run against real Redis                                                  | **Yes**: 1 attempt vs 4 attempts with sleeps 2, 4, 8 s; one DLQ entry; one metric row |
 26: | Job privacy                                                                                                                          | A second user cannot read a job                                                        | **Yes** (404)                                                                         |
 27: | Canary uses numeric version order                                                                                                    | Gateway run with versions v1, v2, v9, v10                                              | **Yes**                                                                               |
 28: | The rollback guard's decisions                                                                                                       | Real `decide()` run with defaults                                                      | **Yes**, including two flaws below                                                    |
 29: | Public model listing hides worker routing                                                                                            | Real response checked                                                                  | **Yes**                                                                               |
 30: | All API responses and WebSocket close codes in [API](api.md)                                                                         | Captured from a running gateway                                                        | **Yes**                                                                               |
 31: | Static validity of code, YAML and shell                                                                                              | Compiled, parsed, `bash -n`                                                            | **Yes**                                                                               |
 32: | Compose and Kubernetes bring-up, real OCR and ASR results, memory figures, latency figures, canary demos, crash recovery on minikube | Project completion reports                                                             | **Reported** only                                                                     |
 33: | Dependency vulnerability status                                                                                                      | No scan was run                                                                        | **Not checked**                                                                       |
 34: 
 35: ## Known issues
 36: 
 37: Found by reading the code and, where noted, by running it. None has been fixed as part of writing this documentation. Severity labels are deliberately avoided; the evidence column is what matters.
 38: 
 39: ### Behaviour of the running system
 40: 
 41: | #   | Issue                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Evidence                                                      | Suggested fix                                                                                                                 |
 42: | --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 43: | 1   | **A full Redis returns HTTP 500 and `/ready` stays 200.** With the Kubernetes setting (200 MB, `noeviction`), the 16th consecutive 10 MiB upload failed                                                        | **Verified**                                                  | Catch the Redis write error and return a deliberate 503; make `/ready` check memory headroom; size Redis for expected uploads |
 44: | 2   | **WebSocket errors return raw exception text**, including the internal worker URL that `/v1/models` hides                                                                                                      | **Verified**                                                  | Map exceptions to fixed messages; log the detail instead                                                                      |
 45: | 3   | **Uploads stay in Redis for an hour after the job finishes** (payload TTL 3600). Results and ownership last two hours                                                                                          | **Verified**                                                  | Delete `payload:{id}` on success and permanent failure                                                                        |
 46: | 4   | **A job for a model whose `worker_name` has no running worker is never processed and never fails.** It shows `queued` until the status key expires, then 404                                                   | Derived from the code; matches the recorded canary demo       | Check for an active consumer group at enqueue time, or add a queue timeout                                                    |
 47: | 5   | **The guard can roll back a healthy canary** right after it is enabled (0, 3 or 9 canary completions with 200 stable)                                                                                          | **Verified**                                                  | Record when the canary last changed and ignore it for one window; until then follow the "wait one window" rule                |
 48: | 6   | **The guard misjudges 100%.** At `canary_percent = 100` with stable still in the window, a healthy canary (500 completions) is "starved: about 10000 expected"                                                 | **Verified**                                                  | Skip the starvation rule at 100%, or use only traffic since the last change                                                   |
 49: | 7   | **The starvation rule is inert at default thresholds for small canaries.** It needs 120 stable completions at 20% (570 at 5%) before it can fire. The recorded demo must have lowered `--min-requests`         | **Verified**                                                  | Document or scale the minimum                                                                                                 |
 50: | 8   | **A model whose only version is a disabled `v2` returns 404, not 503**, and `GET /v1/models/{name}` defaults to `v1`                                                                                           | **Verified**                                                  | Decide 503 vs 404 from all versions, not only `v1`                                                                            |
 51: | 9   | **"Streaming" is whole-file.** The client sends one message; `PredictStream` transcribes each chunk independently; `confidence` is always 0.0; a per-chunk error is returned as transcript text (`[Error: …]`) | Code                                                          | Rename, or implement real chunking                                                                                            |
 52: | 10  | **A transient failure blocks its consumer for up to 14 seconds** because the backoff sleeps inside the single-job loop                                                                                         | Code, and the run showed sleeps 2, 4, 8                       | Schedule retries without sleeping the consumer                                                                                |
 53: | 11  | **Rate limiting is per key, counts rejected requests, and sends no `Retry-After`.** Anyone can mint keys with up to 1000/min each; registration and login are unlimited; Redis down means a 500                | Code; limit bounds **verified**                               | Per-user cap; limit registration and login; return the standard headers                                                       |
 54: | 12  | **The REST size limit is applied after the multipart body is parsed.** No HTTP-level body limit exists                                                                                                         | Code                                                          | Enforce `Content-Length` and a proxy limit                                                                                    |
 55: | 13  | **Retried entries and a backlog can trigger stream trimming of unread jobs** (`MAXLEN ~` is approximate; a retry adds an entry)                                                                                | Code; the Phase 9 test reached 116 entries for a limit of 100 | Alert on backlog; consider a larger limit                                                                                     |
 56: | 14  | **A poison entry that crashes its worker is reclaimed forever** (no delivery-count limit)                                                                                                                      | Documented limitation                                         | Cap deliveries and dead-letter                                                                                                |
 57: | 15  | **Metrics can be lost.** After one failed insert each worker skips for 60 s. WebSocket traffic is not recorded. The ClickHouse DDL is reconstructed and was never checked against a live table                 | Code; reported                                                | Buffer or batch; verify the DDL                                                                                               |
 58: | 16  | **Dead schema and unused code paths.** The `requests` table, `revoked` writes, the `deleted` status, `PAYLOAD_TTL_SECONDS` on workers, and the Kafka modules are never used                                    | **Verified** by search                                        | Remove or implement                                                                                                           |
 59: 
 60: ### Tooling, tests and repository
 61: 
 62: | #   | Issue                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Evidence                                          |
 63: | --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------- |
 64: | 17  | **The two workers' `consumer.py` (about 240 lines) and `metrics.py` are copies**, and the "highest numeric version" rule exists twice                                                                                                                                                                        | Diffed                                            |
 65: | 18  | **The dormant Kafka code has diverged** from the Redis path (no retries, DLQ, owner key, metrics) and `aiokafka` is still installed in three images                                                                                                                                                          | Code                                              |
 66: | 19  | **`ws_latency_compare.py` leaves the model registered as gRPC** and works only on Kubernetes                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Code                                              |
 67: | 20  | **The smoke test's admin step is Compose-only**, its latency is quantised to 2-second polling, and it needs `fixtures/` that are not in the repository. `load-test-vars.csv` is git-ignored                                                                                                                  | Code                                              |
 68: | 21  | **Test gaps:** no end-to-end test; WebSocket success, auth and gRPC streaming untested; ClickHouse never contacted; see [Testing](testing.md#what-is-not-tested)                                                                                                                                             | Read and run                                      |
 69: | 22  | **Tests leave rows in the database** (1 model, 5 users, 4 keys per run)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | **Verified**                                      |
 70: | 23  | **FastAPI `on_event` deprecation warnings** (gateway and ASR worker)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | **Verified** (8 warnings)                         |
 71: | 24  | **No CI/CD pipeline, no image or dependency scanning, no linter or type checker**                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Repository                                        |
 72: | 25  | **Files that exist but were not in the reviewed export:** `inference.proto`, `k8s/secrets.yaml`, `k8s/gateway/jwt-secret.yaml`, `.env.example`, `fixtures/`, `load-test-vars.csv`, `docs/baseline-smoke.txt`. The export script skips names containing `secret` or `.env` and any file type it does not list | Explained by `export_code.py`; confirm they exist |
 73: | 26  | **Documentation debt (resolved by this rewrite):** the previous set quoted an out-of-date test count (75 instead of 102), contained a personal Windows path and a personal interview-prep section, and was about 1,900 lines of agent prompts rather than documentation                                      | Read                                              |
 74: 
 75: ## Limits by design
 76: 
 77: Things the project chose not to build, stated by its own documents and confirmed in the code:
 78: 
 79: - Prometheus, Grafana and a `/metrics` endpoint; ClickHouse in Compose.
 80: - Ingress, TLS, HPA, PodDisruptionBudgets, NetworkPolicies, Helm, a CI/CD pipeline.
 81: - Multi-tenancy, per-model quotas, batching, GPU support, token-level streaming, a result cache, a Postgres request log, a sliding-window rate limiter.
 82: - Canary on the WebSocket path, automatic promotion, statistical testing, notifications.
 83: - Durable Redis on Kubernetes.
 84: - A job replay tool for the dead-letter stream.
 85: - More than two example models.
 86: 
 87: ## How it got here
 88: 
 89: The project was built in five feature phases and then hardened in six more, driven by written prompts and reviewed in writing.
 90: 
 91: | Phase            | Theme                                                               | Outcome (reported)                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Suite                   |
 92: | ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------- |
 93: | 1 to 5           | Gateway, async queue, WebSocket and gRPC, Kubernetes, observability | Built. Phase 5 is partial (about 9 of 12 scoped outcomes): no Prometheus or Grafana                                                                                                                                            | n/a                     |
 94: | 0                | Run and baseline                                                    | First Compose build took about 51 minutes; an invalid upload took 92 s to fail; 20 of 21 tests failed                                                                                                                          | 1 pass                  |
 95: | 6                | Reproducible baseline                                               | Schema, ConfigMap and migration made consistent; dead code removed; smoke test added                                                                                                                                           | n/a                     |
 96: | 7                | Trustworthy tests                                                   | Tests run against the app's own database and Redis                                                                                                                                                                             | 20 + 1 expected failure |
 97: | 8                | Gateway correctness                                                 | Input validation, revoked keys rejected on WebSocket, numeric canary, atomic rate limit, real `/ready`                                                                                                                         | 43                      |
 98: | 9                | Queue and worker reliability                                        | Payload in its own key, retry with backoff, DLQ, crash recovery, supervised containers, probes                                                                                                                                 | 54                      |
 99: | 10               | Metrics pipeline                                                    | One row per finished job, ASR metrics, bounded timeouts                                                                                                                                                                        | 64                      |
100: | 11               | Canary rollback guard                                               | Dry-run CLI that can only reset `canary_percent` to 0                                                                                                                                                                          | 75                      |
101: | Review follow-up | Fixes from a written code review                                    | Timeouts and disable-when-unset, invalid-audio mapping, WebSocket client exit codes, time-based maintenance, WebSocket size check, key limit bounds, hidden routing, generic `/ready` errors, OCR memory limit raised to 3 GiB | **102**                 |
102: 
103: Decisions recorded along the way: documented-but-missing features are corrected in the documents, not built; new versions must match `v<number>`; no canary on the WebSocket path; Kafka stays and is labelled dormant; the guard only ever sets `canary_percent` to 0; the optional guard CronJob was skipped.
104: 
105: The review's findings R1 to R9 were implemented. The ones that can be confirmed from code were: ClickHouse timeouts and disable-when-unset (R1), ASR invalid-audio handling (R2), WebSocket client exit status (R3), time-based maintenance (R4), WebSocket size check on the default path (R5), key rate-limit bounds (R7), hidden internals and generic `/ready` errors (R8), aligned test dependencies (R10), and the OCR memory limit (R12). R6, the guard's warm-up problem, was handled by documentation only and is reproduced above (issue 5).
106: 
107: ## Open work
108: 
109: Suggestions only, roughly in order of value for effort. They are not planned work.
110: 
111: 1. Return a deliberate error when Redis refuses a write, and make `/ready` aware of memory (issue 1).
112: 2. Stop returning raw exception text on the WebSocket (issue 2).
113: 3. Delete the payload when a job finishes (issue 3), and decide how a job for an absent worker should fail (issue 4).
114: 4. Fix the guard's warm-up and 100% edge cases, then consider scheduling it (issues 5 to 7).
115: 5. Add the missing tests: one end-to-end path, WebSocket success and auth, `PredictStream`, the full-Redis case.
116: 6. Security basics from [Security](security.md): TLS, a real `JWT_SECRET`, registration control, a Redis password, non-root containers.
117: 7. Remove or finish the dead code (issues 16 to 18) and merge the duplicated worker modules.
118: 8. Add CI that runs the suite against Postgres and Redis service containers.
119: 9. Re-measure memory and latency with realistic documents and larger samples.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[MD]  docs\testing.md
================================================================================
  1: # Testing
  2: 
  3: What the 102 tests cover, what they do not, what was reproduced for this documentation, and how to use the smoke, load and latency scripts.
  4: 
  5: ## Verification record
  6: 
  7: Reproduced during this review (Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12.3, **Postgres 16** and **Redis 7.0.15** installed locally, the pinned `tests/requirements.txt` installed in a fresh virtual environment, schema built from `db/init.sql` plus both migrations, `TEST_DATABASE_URL` pointing at a scratch database and `TEST_REDIS_URL` at Redis database 1):
  8: 
  9: | Check                                                                                                                                            | Result                                                                                                        |
 10: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 11: | `pytest -q`, first run                                                                                                                           | **102 passed, 8 warnings** in about 3 s                                                                       |
 12: | `pytest -q`, second run                                                                                                                          | **102 passed, 8 warnings** (the suite is repeatable)                                                          |
 13: | Migrations applied on top of `init.sql`                                                                                                          | Only "already exists, skipping" notices (idempotent)                                                          |
 14: | Kubernetes ConfigMap SQL vs `db/init.sql`                                                                                                        | Identical                                                                                                     |
 15: | All Python files compile; all 14 Kubernetes manifests, both Compose files and the load-test file parse as YAML; all shell scripts pass `bash -n` | Yes                                                                                                           |
 16: | `canary_guard.decide()` exercised with defaults                                                                                                  | Table in [Registry and canary](registry-and-canary.md#what-the-decision-function-does-verified-by-running-it) |
 17: 
 18: **Caveat.** `inference.proto` was not in the reviewed export, so I wrote a minimal stand-in from how the code uses the messages (field numbers are arbitrary). The tests passed with it, which shows the code and tests are consistent with each other, but the real proto file was not checked.
 19: 
 20: **Not run:** the Docker images, Docker Compose, Kubernetes, ClickHouse, the real EasyOCR and Whisper models, the smoke test, the load test, and anything needing GPUs. Everything recorded below as "reported" comes from the project's own completion reports, not from this review.
 21: 
 22: The 8 warnings are FastAPI deprecations for `@app.on_event("startup"/"shutdown")` in the gateway and the ASR worker, plus protobuf deprecation notices.
 23: 
 24: ## Running the tests
 25: 
 26: The suite needs a **real Postgres and a real Redis**. Use a scratch database: the tests leave rows behind.
 27: 
 28: ```bash
 29: # 1. Start Postgres and Redis, published on localhost (a Compose overlay does this)
 30: docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.test.yml up -d
 31: 
 32: # 2. Create the scratch database and apply the schema
 33: docker compose exec -T postgres psql -U user -d postgres -c "CREATE DATABASE modelmesh_test;"
 34: docker compose exec -T postgres psql -U user -d modelmesh_test < db/init.sql
 35: for f in migrations/*.sql; do
 36:   docker compose exec -T postgres psql -U user -d modelmesh_test < "$f"
 37: done
 38: 
 39: # 3. Python environment (Python 3.10+, on the Linux filesystem)
 40: python -m venv ~/.venvs/modelmesh && . ~/.venvs/modelmesh/bin/activate
 41: pip install -r tests/requirements.txt
 42: 
 43: # 4. Run. The suite uses Redis database 1.
 44: export TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost/modelmesh_test
 45: export TEST_REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379/1
 46: pytest -q          # then run it a second time to confirm it is repeatable
 47: ```
 48: 
 49: `pytest.ini` sets `asyncio_mode = auto` and `testpaths = tests`, so the scripts in the repository root (`test_ws_client.py`, `ws_latency_compare.py`) are not collected.
 50: 
 51: `tests/conftest.py` generates the protobuf modules into a temporary directory from `inference.proto` at import time (so the proto file must be present), and flushes Redis database 1 before **every** test. It refuses to run that flush against any other Redis database number.
 52: 
 53: ## What the suite covers
 54: 
 55: | File                           |   Tests | Focus                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
 56: | ------------------------------ | ------: | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
 57: | `test_consumer_reliability.py` |      21 | Parametrized over both workers. Permanent vs transient errors, retry then success, real error in the failed status, expired payload, exactly one metric per job, reclaim of stale entries, maintenance driven by elapsed time, and one test with a **real Redis**: a pending job from a "ghost" consumer is reclaimed                                      |
 58: | `test_gateway.py`              |      20 | Atomic rate limit, canary selection (numeric highest and lowest, 0% routes stable, boundary roll, single active version, explicit version bypass), `/ready` behaviour, upload at and over the limit, log redaction of `api_key=`, public model reads hide routing, job lookup by authenticated user, octet-stream worker call, one WebSocket oversize test |
 59: | `test_registry.py`             |      15 | Real-database registry: lookup, listing, disabled and unknown models, version and value validation, duplicate registration, admin-only registration, disable                                                                                                                                                                                               |
 60: | `test_auth.py`                 |      13 | Register, login, keys, revoked keys, short passwords, rate-limit enforcement, key limit bounds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
 61: | `test_canary_guard.py`         |      11 | The pure `decide()` function over many cases, numeric version selection, zero-percent pairs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
 62: | `test_metrics.py`              |       6 | ClickHouse writer: configured host and port, disabled when unset, one warning per back-off window                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
 63: | `test_asr.py`                  |       5 | Transcription, empty audio, invalid audio becomes a permanent input error, HTTP 400                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
 64: | `test_doc_ocr.py`              |       4 | Text and confidence, invalid bytes, octet-stream accepted, HTTP 400                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
 65: | `test_queue.py`                |       3 | Payload stored before the stream entry, job visible only to its owner                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
 66: | `test_ws_client.py`            |       3 | The client script exits with the right status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
 67: | `test_grpc_transport.py`       |       1 | A 6 MiB WAV payload crosses a real gRPC server and client                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
 68: | **Total**                      | **102** |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
 69: 
 70: Counts are the pytest-collected numbers, so parametrized tests count once per case.
 71: 
 72: ### How the tests are built
 73: 
 74: - **Heavy dependencies are stubbed.** The worker tests load `consumer.py` and the HTTP apps through `tests/helpers.py` (`load_module_from_path`) with stand-ins for `inference` and `metrics`, so no model weights are needed. This is also why the dormant Kafka modules are not touched.
 75: - **Real services where it matters:** registry, auth, rate limiting and the ghost-consumer reclaim use the real Postgres and Redis. Most consumer tests use an `AsyncMock` Redis.
 76: - Test dependency versions were aligned with the worker images (NumPy 1.26.4, OpenCV headless 4.10.0.84) after an earlier mismatch.
 77: 
 78: ## What is not tested
 79: 
 80: - **No end-to-end test through real workers.** No test sends a file through the gateway, real Redis, a running worker process and back.
